**FetchGitPack and the gateway mirror**
- **Authorizer:** the pod's pool Runtime, plus, for a commit-SHA want whose url the pod's Runtime lacks, the lineage pool read from the pod-scoped `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:git_lineage` record (§14 rule 7, REG-GITLINEAGE). §14 rule 1 eligibility uses only the pod's pool Runtime. [redesign.2]
- **Lineage record:** written once per restoring bind (resume or 0109 first bind) by CODE-9 `Grant` in `Binder.Resume`, `Binder.Prepare`, and `materializeSlotStages`; replay sets ParentWorkspaceRef too; a Redis loss fails closed and reconstructs from Postgres on the next attempt; tenant pinning stops cross-tenant reads. [redesign.2, non-spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.1.review-reliability.1 (latest loop)]
- **Mirror lock:** every operation holds the url lock shared; concurrent fetches of one commit from one `remote` are single-flight; only capacity deletion (M2) and the M8 purge take it exclusively, by try-lock, never waiting. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1, spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
- **Mirror state:** Ensure fetches `<sha>:refs/lenny/<sha>`; mirrors run with `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false`; the capacity pass runs before Ensure takes the url lock and deletes the requested url last; the mirror root is wiped at `gitref.Mirror` construction. [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
- **Mirror capacity:** the git-mirror emptyDir has no sizeLimit; the gateway enforces `gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes` by LRU deletion; RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED when nothing is deletable. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
- **One Mirror per replica:** CODE-3 rule M2's construction sentence; the binder and the handler share it through `Binder.Mirror`; nil fails closed. [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
- **Limiters:** bare `subsystem.Limiter`s (M3 fetches, M4 pack and walk slots, warm share W = max(1, maxConcurrentPacks − 2) taken before the M4 slot); a walk inside a Pack reuses that Pack's slot; acquisition order has no cycle. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1 (redesign 3 loop), non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]
- **Memo:** per mirror key, a verdict memo and a tree-bytes memo (rule M5); ValidateTree is single-flight per key and runs through neither `UploadGate` nor `gatedExtract`. [redesign.1, non-spec.1.review-single-source.1]
- **ValidateTree listing:** ls-tree for path, mode, and blob size plus a rev-list | cat-file (`%(objecttype) %(objectsize) %(rest)`) listing that also feeds the shallow pack. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1, non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
- **Memory envelope:** creation config `pack.threads=1`, `pack.windowMemory=64m`, `pack.deltaCacheSize=64m`, `core.deltaBaseCacheSize=32m`; step 9 sizes `gateway.resources.limits.memory` from INFRA-1 (g)'s largest limiter-slot peak; dev.yaml drops its 512Mi limit. [non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1, non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
- **Shutdown:** CODE-3 step 6 rules S1 to S4 are constraints with the bound from `--shutdown-timeout`; the means is the implementor's, pinned by TEST-1 item 13. [redesign.reconciliation-2026-10-30c]
- **Status table:** a shutdown-cancelled slot waiter matches only the UNAVAILABLE row; DEADLINE_EXCEEDED is "the caller's context ends, other than by the shutdown step". [non-spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
- **No §28 or RPC row elsewhere:** the §4.7.1 Adapter → Gateway table is the only RPC enumeration; adapter-contract.md mirrors it (DOCS-1 row). [spec.6.review-applicability.1, spec.1.review-edit-sites.1 (latest loop)]
- **Durable state:** the only net-new durable write is the REG-GITLINEAGE ZADD; no etcd write. [spec.1.review-performance.1 (latest loop)]
- **Tier 3 sizing:** claim rate 30/s, 4 Token Service replicas, 5 to 30 gateway replicas; no spec section states an apiserver read budget. [spec.1.review-performance.1, spec.4.review-performance.1]
- **Two GETs per git-https operation:** 0112's keyed read is one uncached `get` per key, so `username` plus `password` cost two; accepted, not filed. [spec.1.review-performance.1 (latest loop)]

**Slot repositories (rules 3 to 7)**
- **Rule 3 construction:** `GIT_DIR=X git init` in a fresh directory outside the build tree, `git config core.bare false`, every rule 3 operation with that git dir and `path` as work tree, then a move to `<path>/.git` replacing any entry; prototyped locally with a planted hook and fsmonitor, nothing fired. - **Runtime-entry removal:** a restore that must fetch a url neither Runtime names fails PERMISSION_DENIED, an accepted failure; the edge row stays conditional on "has to fetch". [spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]

[spec.1.fix-design-G1.1 (second loop), spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]

[spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]

- **Remote-less repository:** the cold gitref.Clone also runs `git init` plus fetch by SHA with no origin, so rule 3 matches it. [spec.1.review-mechanism.1 (latest loop)]

### Traps

- **Do not restore an unconditional symlink admission:** cache-served sources follow `allowSymlinks`. An implementor of ValidateTree will copy extractGitCloneSource's `AllowSymlinks: true`; CODE-3 step 4 carries this trap. [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
- **Do not route ValidateTree through `UploadGate.Do` or `gatedExtract`:** `subsystem.Do` counts every `fn` error as a breaker failure, so size refusals would trip the upload breaker, and `gatedExtract` records the extraction-aborted counter. [redesign.1, non-spec.1.review-single-source.1]
- **Limiters are not Subsystems:** a `subsystem.Subsystem` adds a label outside the §16.1 closed set; the `token_service` Subsystem is not a precedent. A fifth §4.1 subsystem with a breaker, no bound, and the per-url lock as the bound were rejected. [spec.1.fix-G3.1]
- **Do not return to one pack limiter with a pre-fetch-only capacity check:** concurrent fetches of distinct keys grew without bound; M2, M3, and M7 bound them. [redesign.1]
- **Fetches never take the url lock exclusively:** RWMutex writer preference queues every reader behind a waiting Ensure, and a blocking Lock in deletion deadlocks against Pack's nested have-Ensures. Reader-retry or reader-drain protocols were also rejected. The capacity pass runs before Ensure takes the url lock, or the try-lock on the requested url always fails. [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1, non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
- **No sizeLimit, no ENOSPC branch:** a disk-backed emptyDir sizeLimit evicts the replica; do not add one "as a backstop" or a rebuild-once branch. TEST-1 item 22 pins the absence. The LRU pass may consider the requested url, taken last. [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1, non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
- **Accepted mirror residue:** a hot url held at every pass still fails RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED until a free moment, and a repository larger than maxBytes is refetched each time; do not re-file without something smaller than a generation-swap rebuild. [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
- **MISTAKE, M6 replacement fill:** M6 claimed a replacement pod's fill "finds the key already mirrored". M3 cancels a merged fetch when its last waiter returns and git discards the partial pack, so a fetch longer than the fill deadline restarts from zero every round. The remedy is `controller.tuning.gitStoreFillTimeout` plus the INFRA-1 gate; do not detach merged fetches from waiters. [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
- **Warm share before M4:** a warm Pack holding an M4 slot while it waits for the warm share recreates starvation. [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1 (redesign 3 loop)]
- **Memo deadlock rows need a delta Pack:** only a delta Pack reads the memo; warm or shallow Packs start no walk and pass on a deadlocking implementation. Acquire the slot outside the single-flight. [non-spec.3.fix-design-G2.1, non-spec.3.review-reliability.1]
- **Measure summed RSS:** getrusage maxrss and `/usr/bin/time` report one child; the container cgroup charges all; cgroup v2 `memory.oom.group` kills the gateway with its git child. `pack.threads=1` slows the first full fetch, which is why INFRA-1 checks it against the fill deadline. [non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
- **Unpack limit in tests:** a fetch below `transfer.unpackLimit` (100) explodes objects loose; set it to 1 or count from a packet trace. [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
- **Malformed-object row:** the `.git` tree entry is WARN-level and fatal only under `fetch.fsckObjects`; assert the commit object is absent. A malformed `want` must be non-ref syntax (`HEAD~1`), never a hex-length variant. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1, non-spec.2.fix-design-G4.1]
- **Truncated delta pack succeeds:** rule 3 retries with no have, so a truncation row truncates every call. [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
- **gitRef and gitClone.ref diverge on purpose:** the session path pins a SHA with haves; a store fill sends none, so a SHA want gets a shallow pack. [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
- **The CEL rule stays the CRD-time per-entry enforcer:** `applyCRDFields` bypasses the admin validator; only uniqueness moved to `mirror`. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
- **No literal `warming → failed`:** it is false on a preConnect pool; do not name `sdk_connecting → failed` or put a watchdog value into SPEC-2. Re-anchoring the watchdog, rejecting preConnect, capping the fill, and a timeout floor were rejected. A stale reviewer snapshot re-raises this finding; check its quote first. [spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
- **Fill flag on embedded pods:** rendering it on an embedded pod crash-loops the pool (flag.Parse exits 2); it goes to sidecar args only. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
- **Rule 3 must not touch build-tree git state:** do not re-add "remove the `.git` at `path`", which an in-tree symlink an earlier source planted reaches around; a symlink at `path` or a parent lets `git init` reinitialize a hostile repository keeping its hooks. Do not pass a work tree to `git init` (absolute `core.worktree`); `GIT_DIR=X git init` alone sets `core.bare = true`. [spec.5.fix-G1.1, spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
- **Checkout needs `--force`:** seeded or earlier-source files under `path` are untracked relative to the fresh git directory; a non-forced checkout fails every 0109 derive. [spec.5.fix-G1.1]
- **Do not harmonize the 0700 and 0755 git dirs:** CODE-6 bullet (2)'s 0700 dir is deleted per operation; only CODE-5 step 6 promotes. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
- **No runtime-side git in tests:** every adapter-materialized repository (cold included) is UID 65532, so runtime git fails on dubious ownership; `safe.directory=*` is rejected. [non-spec.2.fix-design-G3.1]
- **No presence test on baseCommit:** a delta-built slot holds baseCommit's commit object while trees and history stay store-only, so do not write "holds no object reachable from lenny.baseCommit"; the predicate is "config carries lenny.storeCommit". [spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.1.fix-G1.1 (second loop)]
- **Rule 6 must rewrite storeCommit:** S1 → S2 (delta) → re-checkpoint → restore onto S1 otherwise skips a fetch; TEST-1 item 11 "second restore onto the first store" discriminates. [spec.1.fix-G4.1]
- **Do not graft a shallow frontier:** marking parentless commits shallow masks capture defects; widening the rule 5 copy grows toward the dissociate non-goal; expiring reflogs was rejected. [spec.4.fix-G2.1]
- **Shallow-ancestry edge case with a store present:** the store's history reaches the ancestor, so the failure needs a destination with no store or a delta over the bound. [spec.4.review-mechanism.1, spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
- **No `-c` overrides, no `safe.directory=*`:** a denylist is not fail-closed and `-c` cannot neutralize `include.path`. Every slot write is descriptor-relative (TEST-1 item 8 swap-race row). A `.git` file is skipped. Write `shallow` into the scratch GIT_DIR before `index-pack --strict`. [spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
- **Read-side bound lives in CODE-6 (2):** reads are bounded by copying regular files only; trimming that step loses it. [spec.2.review-security.1]
- **Two 40-hex checks:** rule 1 (`resolvedCommitSha`) and the §13.4 exception (`lenny.baseCommit`) are different rules. [spec.2.review-single-source.1]
- **Do not restore `session_id` on `FetchGitPack` or a derive clause:** it makes the request session-scoped under §4.1 while the warm fill has none, and lets a pod borrow any same-tenant session's lineage. Do not authorize from `pod_assignment` (written after Resume returns), `agent_pod_state` (one session per pod), or the `SandboxClaim`. A derive needs no gateway cold clone, no copied gitClone sources, and no multi-step lineage walk; a legacy checkpoint with no manifest row fails closed. [redesign.2, spec.1.fix-G2.1, spec.1.follow-up-fix.2 (first loop)]
- **Lineage expiry bound:** rule 7 says "that call's bound" with only the resume example (300s); at a first bind it is `maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` or the `/start` window; judged interpretable. [spec.1.review-performance.1 (latest loop)]
- **CODE-9 read-error rule differs from CODE-8 on purpose:** CODE-9 leaves the lineage pool empty on a read error; CODE-8 step 3 fails resolution. The pointer names only the "treats as absent" predicate. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1 (redesign 3 loop)]
- **Do not resolve the pod through `podsession.Registry`:** it holds only this replica's sessions. [redesign.3]
- **Do not restore the dev-path pod claim or an audience-only branch:** one predicate `podIdentityEnforced` keys rules I1, I2, and the rule 5 registration refusal. The trap form is `if podName != "" && podName != req.PodId { deny }`, which skips the binding when VerifyPod finds no extra. [redesign.3, non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
- **MISTAKE, Kind TokenReview row:** TEST-1 item 21 once asserted the pod binding "on Kind's TokenReview"; Kind has no audience and no verifier, so it moved to an envtest VerifyPod row in item 13. Do not set `global.saTokenAudience` in Kind e2e values: the adapter never attaches the SA token and Kind runs without mTLS. A later fix round reintroduced the setting in CODE-4 step 5 (non-spec.1.review-fresh.1, non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1), and the 2026-10-31 decision removed it again; the Kind private rows are skips naming R14. [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1, orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-31]
- **Do not restore the `ReportPodScrub` precedent or a url-only mirror key:** a spoofed `podId` or shared key would leak private objects. [redesign.1]
- **Do not restore the public-only cut, per-provider host binding, or warm-time tenant pinning:** each lost to the 2026-10-30 owner decisions (summary decisions 4, 20, 21). [redesign.1]
- **Do not represcribe the shutdown means:** the sender goroutine, a 5-second `GracefulStop`/`Stop`, and a 6-second test bound are withdrawn; the earlier rejections of a bounded forced stop rested on harms rules S3 and S4 now forbid. The item 13 test needs a pack over the 64 KiB write quota and a consumer that stops after the header. [redesign.reconciliation-2026-10-30c, non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
- **Do not add a second gateway Token Service client or a VCS breaker:** `ReadCredentialSecret` goes through `callTokenService` under `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), c.timeout)`, so a caller cancel never counts against the shared breaker. [redesign.3, non-spec.1.fix-G1.1 (Token Service loop)]
- **Do not re-stage the Token Service Secret path in 0111:** 0112 owns it. The options this run withdrew stay withdrawn there: the live-Role kubectl patch (`helm upgrade` dropped appended names), chart-owned `resourceNames` (superseded by the dedicated namespace), the periodic or startup read with `tokenService.secretResyncInterval` (no defined value on a failed read), and per-site `secretRef` parsing (the parsers diverged). MISTAKE: redesign 3 deleted the periodic re-read without replacing it, which left "the next read" with no trigger; the per-call read later replaced both. [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30d, redesign.token-service-secret-access.apply, spec.1.fix-design-G2.1 (redesign 3 loop)]
- **Rule 4 "fails", never "refuses":** the §4.7.1 row maps rule 4 or 5 refusals to PERMISSION_DENIED while budget exhaustion is RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED; the credential clause keeps "refuses". Do not cite CODE-3 rule M2 or the `git-mirror` volume in spec text. [spec.1.fix-G1.1 (latest loop), spec.4.fix-G1.1]
- **Do not restore tenant-deletion retention:** no retention sentence, no §12.8 erasure-table citation in rule 4, no refuse-on-unregistered check; do not file legal holds or a §12.8 row against the purge. M8 must not wait for the lock. [spec.6.fix-G1.1, spec.6.fix-design-G1.1, spec.8.review-security.1]
- **Item 32 order:** delete acme's pool before acme, because tenant deletion is refused 409 while an owned pool exists. [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1 (redesign 3 loop)]
- **Tier-11 runbook test:** `TestTokenServiceOutageRunbookAttributesToFinalize` requires the runbook to keep `finalize` and CREDENTIAL_MATERIALIZATION_ERROR. [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
- **Embedded Mode versus embedded adapter mode:** `lenny up` k3s and the single-container echo images are different things; pkg/embedded/manifests is a byte-equal render of the chart plus dev.yaml. [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1, non-spec.3.review-mechanism.1]
- **Do not re-file:** the §28 row for FetchGitPack, §29 traces, the cold full-history fetch, co-tenant alternates reads, the full-clone fallback (decision 8), copy cost, the "pool-level template" wording, the `kubeApiServerCIDR` gateway-only wording, the §15.1 SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED "other setup-window failure" wording, the warmup meter gap, the warm-time shared-asset fill (a later step), refuted perf leads (git-host outage, per-call ls-remote, gateway drain kills fills, per-call Secret GET), and gitMirror rows missing from configuration.md. [various]
- **Glob and hook traps:** `*spec-changes.md` also matches `non-spec-changes.md`; a grep with the literal "rm -rf" against spec/ trips the spec hook; qualify 0086 edits by SPEC number. [spec.4.review-applicability.1, spec.4.review-edit-sites.1, spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]

### [spec.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: §6.4 **Private repository stores.** rule 4 states only that each replica deletes mirrors to stay within the §17.8.1 disk budget and that an operation still not fitting fails the fill, materialization, or restore completion; it names no selection predicate — BECAUSE CODE-3 step 7 rule M2 is the one home of which mirror goes (LRU, try-lock exclusive without waiting, requested key last) and TEST-1 item 16 asserts M2; a spec copy contradicted it and would drift — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "mirrors of any key that no operation is using, least recently used first" (a narrower second home missing requested-key-last and the try-lock); dropping the deletion clause (a never-evicting cache would then conform).
DECISION: the edge row "A gateway replica's mirrors exceed the disk budget." is cut to "An operation that does not fit fails (§6.4 **Private repository stores.** rule 4)." — BECAUSE edge rows are pointers [spec.2.fix-G2.1]; deleting the row lost the accepted failure mode.
WATCHOUT: do not write "least-recently-used", "that no operation holds", "requested key last", "any key", or "any tenant" back into rule 4 or the edge row; "any tenant" also omitted public keys, which have no owning tenant — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 Edit (c) rule 4 **Mirror.**; non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 7 rule M2.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G1.1 (latest loop), Standing context "Mirror limits in spec"]: rule 4 no longer "deletes the LRU mirror of any key"; it says the replica deletes mirrors to stay within the budget, and the selection lives only in CODE-3 rule M2. The standing-context line (review-log.md "Mirror limits in spec") should read "deletes mirrors (selection in CODE-3 rule M2)". This shard may not edit review-log.md, so the compaction pass applies it.
FACT: the related-passages lookup on both changed statements returned no proposal citer; hand grep for least.recently|disk budget|LRU|any key|any tenant|does not fit|exceed the disk across the proposal files found only M2 (non-spec :114), TEST-1 item 16 (:299), the §17.8.1 limits row (spec-changes :366), summary item 18, and review-log lines that describe M2, all still true.

### [spec.3.fix-G2.1]

### [spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: §6.4 **Private repository stores.** rule 4 states only that a replica deletes mirrors to stay within the disk budget and fails the operation that still does not fit; it states no selection predicate (which mirror, what order, lock discipline). CODE-3 step 7 rule M2 is the one home of the selection. The edge row "A gateway replica's mirrors exceed the disk budget." keeps only the outcome and the citation — BECAUSE the spec is read without CODE-3, so any predicate written there is a second statement that drifts from M2 (round 1 wrote "LRU of any key", which deletes in-use mirrors and the requested key first) — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "mirrors of any key that no operation is using, least recently used first" restates M2 in spec, a narrower copy of the same claim that drifts the next time M2 changes; deleting the eviction clause entirely lets a spec-only reader build a cache that never evicts and fails permanently once full.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G1.1 (latest loop)]: the Standing context line "Mirror limits in spec" says rule 4 "deletes the LRU mirror of any key". After this round rule 4 says the replica deletes mirrors to stay within the budget, and the selection (LRU among mirrors whose lock it takes exclusively without waiting, requested key last) lives only in CODE-3 step 7 rule M2.
WATCHOUT: do not put "least-recently-used", "no operation holds", or "any tenant/any key" back into rule 4 or the edge row; mirror selection is M2's. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md rule M2 (line ~114), spec-changes.md rule 4 (line ~107)

### [spec.3.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: the keep-later merge for the lineage record has one home, the SPEC-10 Edit (a) §12.4 `git_lineage` key row ("A write drops expired members and keeps the later of a pool's recorded and new expiry, and the key expires with its latest member."). Rule 7 stays as written, because it states the expiry a write carries and already cites §12.4 for the record — BECAUSE the merge is a property of the store, and CODE-9 step 2 plus TEST-1 item 34 already implement and test exactly that — ALTERNATIVES: restating "at least that call's bound" in rule 7 (a second statement of one rule); a per-session member key such as pool|session (larger structure, changes CODE-9 and the FetchGitPack read for no gain).
DECISION: the §28.3 REG-GITLINEAGE Content cell drops "each until its restoring call's bound" and keeps the rule 7 pointer — BECAUSE with a shared member the pool lasts until the latest of its restoring calls' bounds, so the clause describes lifetime imprecisely, and lifetime is owned by rule 7 plus §12.4.
WATCHOUT: one sorted-set member per pool name means two sessions on one pod with the same lineage pool share a member; any plain ZADD (no GT) shortens the first session's window (resume 300s after a first bind at maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds 600s). EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md "`resuming` timeout (300s)" and "`maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` ... (default: 600s)".

### [spec.3.review-security.1]
- FACT: the pod-scoped lineage record (REG-GITLINEAGE) cannot leak a tenant-owned lineage pool to another tenant through pod reuse, because a reused pod is tenant-pinned for its lifetime on every pool, including one with `recycle.allowCrossTenantReuse`, and the platform never resets the pin. A non-recycling pod serves one session. FetchGitPack has no per-call tenant check, and this pin is the control that makes that safe. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454, :459, :469
- WATCHOUT: a `gitClone` with no `auth` whose `ref` is a 40-hex SHA skips ls-remote (spec/14_workspace-plan-schema.md:92). On a tenant-owned pool with a private entry for that url, rule 1 eligibility (url match only) therefore serves private content through the owner tenant's credential. That is the same tenant, and the tenant-level `credentialPolicy` (spec/04_system-components.md:1790, :1811) has no per-user grant that this bypasses, so it is not a security regression. Do not file it unless a per-user or per-session VCS grant is added to the spec. — EVIDENCE: spec/14_workspace-plan-schema.md:92
- DECISION: no security findings in round 3 — BECAUSE the round-2 diff only adds the rule 4 disk-budget/LRU sentences and an edge row; cross-tenant LRU eviction is availability, not a security bound — ALTERNATIVES: filing the cross-tenant eviction as a noisy-neighbour finding was rejected under "merely less strict".

Do not file "lineage record leaks to the next session" as a cross-tenant issue. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454, :469

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: rule M5's shared ValidateTree walk waits for its M4 slot and runs under rule M3's merged-fetch context (a pointer; M3 stays the one home of the last-waiter context) — BECAUSE the binder's context is the /start launch context, context.WithTimeout(r.Context(), launchBound) (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go launchContext), so a walk on the leader's context fails every follower when the leader's deadline passes or its client disconnects — ALTERNATIVES: generalizing the last-waiter rule into M6 (moves text to fix an ambiguity nobody filed); restating M3's clause in M5 (second copy goes stale); each caller taking its own M4 slot before joining (breaks M5's hold-and-wait ordering and still runs the walk on one caller's context).
WATCHOUT: golang.org/x/sync/singleflight's Do runs fn on the leader's goroutine and context, which is exactly the defect; the tree has no singleflight helper (grep pkg/ cmd/ empty), so the implementor writes a ref-counted waiter context like M3's merged fetch. The TEST-1 item 20 (M5) row's discriminating case needs the M4 slot held so the leader is cancelled while the walk is still waiting. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 7 rules M3 and M5; TEST-1 item 20 (M5)
FACT: review-log standing context "Acquire the slot outside the single-flight" means callers do not take a slot before joining; it stays true with the M5 edit. — EVIDENCE: review-log.md Standing context, "Memo deadlock rows need a delta Pack"

FACT: Every podsession.ResolvePool caller in the tree is in the CODE-8 step 3 list, ResolveReadoptPool included. ### [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-31]
DECISION: R14, option A. Private repository stores stay in 0111, and plan step R14 (agent-pod mTLS client identity: the adapter's TLS GatewayControl dial and the GatewayControl SA token row) is a recorded prerequisite for private-store serving to take effect in a deployment. Edits: CODE-4 drops `tests/testinfra/kind/e2e-values.yaml` (`global.saTokenAudience`) from its targets, and step 5 keeps the TLS-gated token attach, states that a plaintext dial appends no token option, keeps the Kind audience unset, and names R14 as the prerequisite. The TEST-1 preamble runs the private rows at tiers 3 and 4 with the TokenReview fake and makes every Kind private row a `t.Skip` whose reason opens with the `blocked:` category of `cmd/lenny-test/skipreason` and names R14, so `tests/registers/skip-reasons.yaml` gains no entry. Item 21 lists its private rows as those skips, and item 32 runs the same private rows at tier 4. Summary: the `global.saTokenAudience` defect bullet now states the plaintext agent dial and the Kind break; Decision 25, the "What changes" private bullet, an R14 row in Impacts, and the CODE-4 index entry are updated. Checklist: an external-prerequisites line, and the S19 and S22 lines. — BECAUSE CODE-4 step 5 set the Kind audience while Kind runs `mtls.enabled: false`, so every tokenless GatewayControl call on Kind would be refused with `token_missing` (non-spec.1.review-applicability.1, non-spec.1.review-fresh.1, non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1), and the controller renders no adapter TLS flags in any deployment, which is R14's scope (gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md R14) — ALTERNATIVES: moving private stores to a follow-up proposal (rejected: the private contract, its tests, and its mirror keying are staged and reviewed here, and R14 changes none of them); attaching the token on a plaintext dial (rejected: it sends a bearer token in cleartext and gRPC fails the call, because `SATokenCredentials` requires transport security); enabling mTLS on Kind in 0111 (rejected: it needs the per-pod certificate R14 adds). Decided by the orchestrator for the owner under standing delegation.
DECISION: the `lenny/vcs_token` rate limit. 0111 stays as staged with no limiter. The exposure is filed in the summary's "Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage" list as a follow-up on 0112's Token Service read path, and open decision 1 moved into summary Decision 26; the open-decisions section now states that none remain. — BECAUSE a limiter adds a tool rate-limit contract the spec does not state, and 0112 shares one `token_service` breaker by decision — ALTERNATIVES: a per-pod or per-session limit in CODE-7 step 4 (Alternative A in the former open decision); excluding pod-driven reads from the breaker count (Alternative B, ruled out by §4.3 **High availability and failure handling:** and 0112's shared-breaker decision). Decided by the orchestrator for the owner under standing delegation. [f1.human-decisions]
DECISION: the stale `non-spec.3.*` ledger entries were deleted. They reviewed the pre-0112 CODE-7 rules T1 to T5, the promemit `secret_name` label, the Token Service Role render, `ReadCredentialSecret`, and TEST-1 items 33 to 35, 39, and 40 as they stood before 0112 took the Token Service Secret path; none of that text is in the current staging, and TEST-1 items 33 to 35 now carry other subjects. The Standing context tags that cite `non-spec.3.*` resolve to the archive. — BECAUSE the halted run's next steps asked for it, and a later lens would otherwise review deleted text.
FACT: the Standing context Open item "UNVERIFIED Token Service content left in non-spec staging" is closed as absent. non-spec-changes.md contains no rule T1 to T5, no `ReadCredentialSecret`, no `promemit`, no Token Service Role render, and no TEST-1 item 38 or later (TEST-1 ends at item 37). Its only Token Service mentions are the CODE-7 preamble pointer to 0112, the CODE-7 step 6 `values.yaml` comment, and item 21's keyed-read sentence, all of which consume 0112's path. — EVIDENCE: grep over non-spec-changes.md for `T1`, `rule T`, `ReadCredentialSecret`, `promemit`, `credentialSecretRefs`, and `Token Service`.
FACT: the non-spec.1.review-fresh.1 UNVERIFIED (production agent pods dial GatewayControl in plaintext) is the R14 scope: the plan's R14 entry states that the podspec emits no TLS flags and mounts no certificate material, so the decision above records it rather than leaving it open. — EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md "R14. Agent-pod mTLS client identity" **Scope.**

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: closes UNVERIFIED "tenant id reuse". A deleted tenant cannot hand its id, and so a private mirror key, to a new tenant. The tenant record persists: `state='deleted'` from the Phase-6 tombstone onward (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:890), the bootstrap seed refuses an entry naming a `deleting` or `deleted` tenant (spec/17_deployment-topology.md:490), and §10.2 treats `deleting`/`deleted` as not registered (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:243). Tenant deletion is also blocked while any pool exists under the tenant (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1341), and SPEC-2 rule 2 counts owned pools, so no pool can authorize a FetchGitPack for the key by the time the purge runs. — EVIDENCE: lines cited.
FACT: closes UNVERIFIED "purge cadence" (spec half). §12.9 T4 erasure is "Immediate" (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:1098), and the tenant deletion SLA for T4 is 4 hours across all six phases (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:944). Rule 4's purge fires once the tenant is not registered, which happens at Phase 3, before Phase 4 deletes data. A 30 s sweep meets both. Whether every gateway configuration wires `tenantRegistered` is code-side and belongs to the non-spec loop.
FACT: closes UNVERIFIED "T4 scope". §12.9 enforces classification controls at the §12.6 storage interface boundary (spec/12_storage-architecture.md "**Enforcement.**" paragraph under the controls table). The gateway mirror, like the agent pod's workspace emptyDir, is not a §12.6 store. Rule 4 adopts only the erasure timeline for workspace files, so it gives the mirror no weaker treatment than a pod workspace. This is not a regression and should not be filed.
FACT: the gitClone.url restrictions paragraph (spec/14_workspace-plan-schema.md:80) has only the `^https://` and parse rules and no SSRF host filter, so the gitRepository entry adopting "the `^https://` rule" keeps parity with gitClone. Do not file a fill-path SSRF bypass.
FACT: a tenant-owned pool that points at a Runtime carrying another tenant's private url leaks nothing, because rule 4 resolves the credential for the pool's `ownerTenantId` and never for the entry author's tenant.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
- DECISION: staged §6.4 rule 2 stays unedited; CODE-8 step 1 states that rule 2 reads the merged pool — BECAUSE the write registration judges on a partial PUT is the merged pool, which carries the stored owner, so an owned pool's unrelated PUT is admitted and `{"ownerTenantId": ""}` on an owned pool is a rejected change — ALTERNATIVES: rewording rule 2 reopens the converged spec loop without need; a strict decoder fails a client that PUTs a GET body back; a separate owner route adds surface for a field that never changes.
- DECISION: `UpdatePoolRequest.ownerTenantId` is `*string` — BECAUSE `applyPoolUpdateMerge` applies only non-nil pointers, and without the field `handleUpdatePool` decodes non-strictly and drops the value with a success response — ALTERNATIVES: see above.
- DECISION: CODE-8 step 3 has no soft-deleted branch — BECAUSE §6.4 rule 3 already gives the outcome on resume and re-adoption, and a branch can only repeat or diverge from it — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "is set and differs from `tenantID`" repeats the rule; blocking `handleDeletePool` does not close the contradiction and is filed as a summary defect.
- WATCHOUT: do not add an `IsActive` filter to `PoolPolicy` to match its doc comment; the owner would vanish and an owned pool would resolve for every tenant. The comment is the defect — EVIDENCE: `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go` `PoolPolicy` has no `IsActive` check, and both `poolstore` `Get`s return soft-deleted rows (`pgstore.go` `Get` comment "callers consult Pool.IsActive()"; `Memory.Get` in `poolstore.go`).
- FACT: `handleDeletePool` soft-deletes with no dependents check, so §15.1's Pool block (running or suspended sessions) is unenforced and running sessions can sit on a soft-deleted pool. BUILD-GAPS F-4.6.39 covers only the CRD pair; the summary defects list now carries the gap — EVIDENCE: `pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/pools.go` `handleDeletePool`; spec/15_external-api-surface.md **Deletion semantics for resources with dependents.**
- CORRECTS [non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1]: the UNVERIFIED on item 33 is closed. A soft-deleted pool owned by the row's tenant is now a lineage pool (P), which matches a soft-deleted unowned pool and the §14 rule 7 owner clause.
- OPEN: this fix group took no action on the caller's carried items (TEST-1 item 8 symlinked `.git/objects` row, Mirror wiring row, first-bind restore row, and the tenant-id reuse, §12.9 T4 scope, and 30 s purge UNVERIFIEDs), because they are outside G1's findings.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]
- DECISION: CODE-4 step 2.1 stages no gRPC keepalive on the adapter's GatewayControl dial (sentence deleted) — BECAUSE with no client pings there are no ping strikes, so a FetchGitPack silent for minutes has no transport failure mode, and the fill deadline already bounds a stall; a half-open connection is detected only at that deadline, which is accepted — ALTERNATIVES: client 10s/5s plus a server EnforcementPolicy adds two options and a way for a ping timeout to cut every RPC on the pod's one LNK-GWCONTROL connection, with no spec basis; a 5m client interval serves nothing against a 15m deadline and sits on the default MinTime strike boundary.
- WATCHOUT: do not add keepalive to `gatewaycontrol.Dial` alone: the server sets no EnforcementPolicy, so grpc's defaults (MinTime 5m, maxPingStrikes 2) send GOAWAY too_many_pings after about 40 s of silence at a 10s interval, and CODE-4 step 4 makes that fatal — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go newGatewayControlServer opts; grpc@v1.80.0/internal/transport/defaults.go and http2_server.go.

DECISION: every failure inside CODE-5 step 6 (fetcher, git, filesystem) wraps `workspace.ErrSlotRepository`, and `FinalizeWorkspace` answers it `codes.Unavailable`, `tracing.CategoryTransient` — BECAUSE the default case answers InvalidArgument, `SlotBindError.Reason` turns that into non-retryable `workspace_validation`, and §5.2 reserves that reason for a plan invalid on any slot — ALTERNATIVES: reusing `errRestore` (unexported in package adapter, message names a snapshot restore); passing the fetcher's gRPC code through (RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED would classify as `SlotReasonOOM` and skip a retry); transient for fetch only (a truncated pack and ENOSPC are equally unrelated to plan validity).

That costs one needless slot retry and is accepted; the materialization still fails, so item 16's deleted-commit row and the spec-changes Edge cases row on a revoked credential stay true. The workspace package never receives `Server.StagingDir`, so the old text also needed new plumbing. Do not re-add a failure-removal clause. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slot.go `slotlayout.RemoveTree(st.paths)`; pkg/adapter/staging.go `workspaceRoot, stagingDir := entry.paths.Current, entry.paths.Staging`.

Both sit on the workspace volume, so the rename to `<path>/.git` stays on one filesystem. ### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: The Compose gateway needs only the Dockerfile.git build entry, with no `git-mirror` volume and no `--git-mirror-dir` flag — BECAUSE Compose runs with no `--agent-namespace`, so `podBinder` is nil and `extractGitCloneSource`/`CloneArchive` are unreachable; its only git use is the ls-remote `RefResolver` at plan pinning — ALTERNATIVES: a Compose volume or flag for `<root>/tmp` (dead configuration, since Compose never reaches the binder).
FACT: The binder is built only inside the `*agentNamespace != ""` block; the sessionsrv ref resolver is wired unconditionally. docker-compose.yml's other `build:` entry is lenny-migrate, and compose/*.yml have no `build:` entry. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:153,297; cmd/lenny-gateway/sessionsrv.go:195; docker-compose.yml:104,119
DECISION: source-hash.sh gets no test row — BECAUSE the script has no harness and `Dockerfile.supervisor`, the precedent name, has none either; a string grep would pin the spelling rather than the rebuild — ALTERNATIVES: a tier-0 grep (weak); a shell harness (out of proportion to one `-name` term).
WATCHOUT: `Dockerfile.git` in the build-images.sh third field must keep the second field: an empty `binary` drops `--build-arg BINARY`, and Dockerfile.git needs it. The script's header comments (lines 11-12 and 36-38) say a Dockerfile-naming entry builds with no BINARY arg, which CODE-1 makes false; the implementor updates them in the same edit. — EVIDENCE: scripts/cloud/aws/build-images.sh:36-38,106-109
WATCHOUT: `--changed` does not select compose_mode on a docker-compose.yml edit, because tests/change-graph.json has no such key and `docker-compose.yml` is absent from `knownFiles`; the S13 tier list is what makes the tier-3 run happen. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-test/cmd_validate.go:1064-1068
OPEN: CODE-3 step 7 rule M2 says each replica constructs one `gitref.Mirror` and deletes the root's contents, and neither it nor step 9 says whether construction is gated on `--agent-namespace` or what a failed construction does. Compose and `make run` have no git-mirror volume, and the `--git-mirror-dir` default `/var/lib/lenny/git-mirror` is not creatable by UID 65532 or an ordinary developer. An unconditional, fatal construction would stop both gateways booting. This is independent of G2; a later round should state that the Mirror is constructed where the binder is, or that construction failure is non-fatal outside it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.2]
DECISION: delete "Run the call with gRPC keepalive on the connection." from CODE-4 step 2.1 and stage no client keepalive and no server enforcement policy — BECAUSE with no client pings there are no ping strikes, so a silent FetchGitPack has no transport failure mode, and the fill deadline already bounds a stall; the spec states keepalive only for the gateway-to-pod direction (§10.1, §11.3), so the other direction would need a spec edit no finding forces — ALTERNATIVES: 10s/5s client params plus `KeepaliveEnforcementPolicy` on newGatewayControlServer (new behaviour on every GatewayControl RPC of every adapter binary with no spec basis, and a new way to cut the one connection); client `Time` of 5m against the default MinTime (no purpose, the interval sits on the strike boundary).
DECISION: a half-open connection to a dead gateway node stalls its fills until the 15m deadline, then takes the pod to `failed`. This is accepted and is not staged: non-spec-changes CODE-4 step 2.1 already calls the deadline the only bound, and every other GatewayControl RPC has the same exposure today. A re-filed "no liveness on the fill stream" finding should be answered from this entry.
WATCHOUT: do not re-add keepalive to `gatewaycontrol.Dial` without the server policy. A 10s client interval draws GOAWAY `too_many_pings` after about 40 s of silence — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go newGatewayControlServer opts; grpc@v1.80.0 internal/transport/http2_server.go handlePing, maxPingStrikes = 2, defaults.go defaultKeepalivePolicyMinTime.

WATCHOUT: lineage-pool mirror-key rows discriminate only on private entries. Two public entries share the key ("", url), so "served under B's mirror key" and "A is used" are unobservable on public entries; assert which owner's credential resolved — EVIDENCE: CODE-3 step 7 rule M1.
FACT: a total TokenReview outage is refused by the stream interceptor with `Unauthenticated` (reason=tokenreview_unavailable) before the handler runs, so the status table's UNAVAILABLE cell is reached only when `VerifyPod` fails after the interceptor's `Verify` passed. Each call makes two reviews — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/satoken.go RequireSATokenInterceptor.
CORRECTS [the G3 finding "Fail-closed cells of the FetchGitPack status table and rule I2 have no row"]: its suggested row "a failing TokenReview answers UNAVAILABLE" fails on a fake that fails every review; the row needs a fake that fails only the handler's review.
CORRECTS [the G3 finding "The §4.7.1 ref-resolution and streaming clauses have no row"]: its suggested chunk-size row does not pin the streaming clause, because a handler that buffers the whole pack still emits 1 MiB chunks. The discriminator is bounded heap growth with the consumer stopped.
FACT: `workspaceplan.IsCommitSHA` is the existing 40-char lowercase hex predicate, already used by gitref.Clone and CODE-2 step 2 — EVIDENCE: pkg/workspaceplan/refresolve.go.
OPEN: `haves` has no count bound, and each unknown have costs one remote fetch under the owner's credential (CODE-3 step 5), up to the gRPC message size. No path in the proposal sends more than one or two haves. Decide in a later round whether to cap it or list it in the summary's defects; G3's fix does not touch it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: slot construction builds its git directory under the `stagingDir` MaterializeWithPolicy receives, with no explicit failure removal — BECAUSE `slotlayout.RemoveTree` deletes the slot root at slot cleanup, the §4.7.1 stamp-once rule means a new bind attempt reaches the slot only after the entry is removed, and the workspace package never receives `Server.StagingDir`, so the old location also needed new plumbing — ALTERNATIVES: keep `/workspace/.staging` and remove on every failure path (leaves a pod-wide location and new plumbing); both location and removal (a lower rung already closes it).
DECISION: every failure inside CODE-5 step 6 wraps one exported sentinel `workspace.ErrSlotRepository`, answered `codes.Unavailable` with `CategoryTransient` as `errRestore` is — BECAUSE slotfailure.go defaults to transient since a needless retry costs one attempt, and restore completion already flattens every FetchGitPack status the same way — ALTERNATIVES: reuse `errRestore` (unexported in package adapter, and workspace cannot import adapter); pass the fetcher's gRPC code through (RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED would read as SlotReasonOOM, PERMISSION_DENIED as a non-retryable policy rejection outside the prep stage).
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]: Settled "Promoted `.git` mode" names the 0700 `/workspace/.staging`. After this round the build location is the slot's `/workspace/slots/{sessionId}/staging`, also 0700 (slotlayout.StagingMode). CODE-6 bullet (2)'s per-operation scratch directory stays in `Server.StagingDir`.
WATCHOUT: `workspace_validation` is reachable from any non-errRestore MaterializeWithPolicy error, because FinalizeWorkspace's default case is InvalidArgument; a new fallible step in the materializer needs a deliberate classification — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/staging.go:318-329, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotfailure.go:97-100.

FACT: the reflog-only shallow case is `commit --amend` then `reset` back to baseCommit: the amended commit is then reachable only from the reflog, and its parent is the missing ancestor. The amend-only form was reproduced earlier; the reset form is reasoned from rev-list parent traversal and was not run, because this agent is read-only — EVIDENCE: review-log-archive.md:305.
FACT: the caller's test-coverage gaps are already staged, so Open "Mirror wiring test", Open "First-bind restore test row", and Deferred D6 can retire: item 4 holds the nil Mirror and shared-Mirror rows, item 11 holds the first-bind ordering and `current`-unchanged rows, and item 8 lists `objects` among the symlinked directories — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 items 4, 8, and 11.

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance finding filed. The only net-new durable write is still the REG-GITLINEAGE script per restoring bind, and there is no etcd write. TokenReview creates are not persisted. Rule P1's Sandbox, SandboxWarmPool, and SandboxTemplate reads go through an uncached `client.New` client, so they add no informer and no watch memory — BECAUSE cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:176 builds `client.New(cfg, client.Options{Scheme: scheme})` and the gateway Role grants get on those resources (charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:188,197) — ALTERNATIVES: filing per-call apiserver GETs and TokenReviews (no spec apiserver budget exists; earlier refuted leads).
FACT: TokenReviewVerifier performs a TokenReview on every call, with no cache. An I1 `VerifyPod` that does not reuse the interceptor's review therefore costs two TokenReviews per FetchGitPack. That is below the bar at about 60 calls/s at Tier 3. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/satoken_verify.go:44-48
FACT: closes UNVERIFIED "tenant id reuse". A deleted tenant keeps a tombstone row, and creating a tenant with that id returns 409 RESOURCE_ALREADY_EXISTS, so a re-registered id cannot inherit a private mirror key. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenants.go:1132-1136,1217-1225
FACT: §12.9 T4 is opt-in through tenant `dataClassification.workspaceTier` and covers "workspace files, snapshots, and session transcripts". Its erasure cell reads "Immediate + cryptographic erasure where supported". Whether mirror objects count as workspace files remains for the security lens. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:1083-1098
DECISION: did not file the unbounded mirror pack count (review-log.md:474 UNVERIFIED). It accumulates one pack and one ref per distinct ensured commit under gc.auto=0, and a gateway restart wipes the mirror. It has no quantified degradation against a spec budget — BECAUSE review-log-archive.md:1107 judged it below the bar and nothing has changed since — ALTERNATIVES: a periodic repack under the exclusive try-lock (adds a mechanism the lock model would need to absorb).
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 8]: filed this round as a missing-test finding (symlinked `.git/objects` row, D6).
CORRECTS [Open "Mirror wiring test"]: closed. TEST-1 item 4 now asserts `w.podBinder.Mirror` is the handler's `*gitref.Mirror`, plus the nil-Mirror fail-closed row. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:278
USEFUL [Standing context "Do not re-file" refuted perf leads]: kept me off the outage and ls-remote leads.

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.2]
DECISION: filed three findings: the GatewayControl keepalive enforcement gap, the warm-fill sustained-rate gap in rule M6 and INFRA-1, and the absent transport retry on a session-time FetchGitPack — BECAUSE each changes a staged outcome at Tier 3 or on a rollout — ALTERNATIVES: did not file CODE-8's mirror read error, because shipped foldPoolPolicy already fails resolution on that read (resolve.go:393-395), so Postgres failover is no worse; CODE-8 adds only one read per extra candidate.
FACT: GatewayControl's grpc.NewServer sets no keepalive option, so grpc-go's default policy applies: MinTime 5m, PermitWithoutStream false, more than 2 strikes sends GOAWAY too_many_pings and closes the connection. Strikes reset only after the server writes headers or data, so a silent wait counts. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:145-188; grpc@v1.80.0/internal/transport/defaults.go:40, http2_server.go:864-911
FACT: LNK-GWCONTROL is one connection per pod process to one replica through the ClusterIP, so a pod's FetchGitPack reaches that replica, usually a different one from the binder's. A pinned commit is therefore ensured on the binder's replica and on the pod's replica. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:108
FACT: `/finalize` and delegated-child materialization get one attempt and no replacement pod, so the review-log-archive.md:1683 claim that every caller falls onto a retry path is false for them, and that FACT's drain premise was already corrected at :1856. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:303
UNVERIFIED: ResolveRef's ls-remote holds no slot (rule M3), so n concurrent warm fills run n git plus git-remote-https process pairs outside rule M7's sum, while step 3 says each git process the mirror runs stays inside M7's peaks. LsRemoteResolver also buffers the whole ref advertisement. Not filed, because the per-process RSS is unmeasured here. INFRA-1 phase (g) is the place to measure it.
WATCHOUT: any new keepalive on `gatewaycontrol.Dial` affects every adapter RPC, and the server policy must ship with it or the connection dies after about 40 s of silence at a 10 s interval. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:330-333 (the mirror-image fix, F-11.3.12)
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-performance.1 "tenant id reuse" and "Mirror wiring test"]: both still hold; TEST-1 items 4, 8, and 11 now carry the three caller-named rows, so nothing there needs filing.

### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]

FACT: Tenant ids are never reused. Deletion tombstones the row (`state = 'deleted'`, `deleted_at` set), the only hard `DELETE FROM tenants` is the preflight-tenant down migration, and `Create` on an existing id returns `ErrAlreadyExists` (23505). A re-registered id therefore cannot inherit a purged private mirror key. This closes the UNVERIFIED "tenant id reuse" item. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:235-259, :400-402; migrations/0096_preflight_tenant.down.sql:7; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenants.go:1217-1225
FACT: Every gateway configuration wires a tenant store (Postgres or in-memory), so M8's `tenantRegistered` always has one. This closes the wiring half of "UNVERIFIED purge cadence and wiring"; the T4 "Immediate" half stays with the security lens. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:278, :293; cmd/lenny-gateway/auth_seams.go:71-80
FACT: `resumeOnPod` runs once per resume attempt (resume_driver.go:153 calls releaseThenResumeOnPod), so CODE-9's `lineagePool` re-reads Postgres on every attempt, and the §12.4 "reconstructed on the next attempt" clause holds by construction. No test pins it, which is filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:61-117; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go:153
FACT: A stale lineage grant left on a pod after a failed bind cannot leak across tenants. The tenant pin applies on every pool, and rule 7 drops a lineage pool owned by another tenant. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1403-1409; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454
CORRECTS [Open "Mirror wiring test"]: this is closed. TEST-1 item 4 now has the nil-`Mirror` binder row and the `w.podBinder.Mirror` same-instance wiring row.

No row covers CODE-6 step 3 (first-bind completion runs before plan sources, and `current` is unchanged on failure). Both are filed this round. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:286, :292, :234

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 item 6 drops every directory-leftover assertion. The row asserts `codes.Unavailable` (CODE-5 step 6) as the discriminating property, and `current` byte-identical as the observable outcome, for a fetcher failing `PERMISSION_DENIED` and for every pack failing `index-pack` — BECAUSE the failed construction deliberately leaves `<slot>/staging` for slot cleanup, so no staging directory has a checkable post-failure state — ALTERNATIVES: retargeting the assertion at the slot's `stagingDir` (contradicts the no-failure-removal decision); deleting only the clause (leaves the git half of the sentinel unpinned).
DECISION: the fetcher row uses `PERMISSION_DENIED` in place of `UNAVAILABLE` — BECAUSE an implementation passing the fetcher's gRPC code through, the alternative [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1] rejected, answers `Unavailable` on an `UNAVAILABLE` fetch and passes that row — ALTERNATIVES: listing both codes (the `UNAVAILABLE` case is dominated). Item 36's `PERMISSION_DENIED` asserts the fetch's answer, so it does not conflict.
DECISION: no filesystem-failure case is added to item 6 — BECAUSE CODE-5 step 6 names no filesystem seam and the finding names only the git half; an unprivileged-mode trick would be root-sensitive in CI — ALTERNATIVES: inventing a seam for one row.
FACT: `current` byte-identical holds on this path without new code: `buildResolvedTree` clears the build tree on a source failure and `promoteStaging` runs only after it returns. CODE-5 step 6 does not state this, so the row must not attribute it to that step. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go `MaterializeWithPolicy`, `buildResolvedTree`; pkg/adapter/staging_restore_test.go `TestFinalizeWorkspaceRestoreFailuresFailTheBindAndLeaveCurrent_spec_7_1`.
WATCHOUT: write "every pack" rather than "a pack": a store-backed slot whose delta fails retries once, so a single corrupt pack can succeed and the row would assert the wrong answer. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 6.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: rule M2 now states the construction site, gate, and failure rule: `buildPodLifecycle`'s `--agent-namespace` block, fatal on failure, none without the flag. `--git-mirror-dir` keeps the chart path as its default, and `adminCluster.gatewayOptions` passes its own directory — BECAUSE a Mirror then exists exactly when a cluster client does, so rule P1 replaces any nil arm in the handler, and a broken chart volume fails at startup, which keeps M2's wipe a guarantee — ALTERNATIVES: empty flag default with a second gate, lazy root preparation, non-fatal construction, and a default under os.TempDir (two gateways would wipe each other); see [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1].
DECISION: the default gateway CPU limit is deleted from values.yaml, presets/dev.yaml, and e2e-values.yaml, and rule M7 and INFRA-1 phase (g) stay as written — BECAUSE the M3 and M4 limiters bound the git children and a CFS quota adds a pause with no bound — ALTERNATIVES: a sized limit of request + fetches + packs (10.25 cores at the defaults, re-derived on every concurrency change, and forces edits to M7's label and scope line); see [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1].
WATCHOUT: `adminCluster.gatewayOptions` appends its flags after the caller's `args`, and Go's flag package keeps the last value, so item 16's fatal row loses a `--git-mirror-dir` passed in `args` once the helper sets a temporary directory. The row has to set its value after the helper's, or the gateway starts and `runGatewayToExit` reports it still running. The helper has no `t`, and it has two call sites, one in a loop. USEFUL [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]: its site list and its ban on a tier 1 fatal row held on re-verification, and it saved the sweep over Makefile, Compose, and the tier 4 harness.
