### [non-spec.4.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: applied the design as written: **Bind deadline.** in CODE-5 step 2 is the one home, M6 points at it, and step 5 carries the deadline to the adapter fetcher — BECAUSE `handleFinalize` and `mintClaimStartPersist` ran the bind on bare `r.Context()`, so M6's stated bound did not exist on either path — ALTERNATIVES: plain `launchContext` on `/finalize`, rejected because the handler then beats the 5 s watchdog tick and the call answers 503 with no reason; the other alternatives are in non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1.
FACT: nothing under `prepareAtFinalize` or `stageWorkspace` spawns a goroutine or detaches from ctx, so cancelling the bound context as soon as `prepareAtFinalize` returns ends no live work — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/{bindprepare,workspacestage,archiveextract}.go have no `go func`, `WithoutCancel`, or `context.Background`.
FACT: a /finalize that loses to the watchdog answers 409 because `finalizePrepareFailed` runs `failFinalizing` on `r.Context()` and renders its precondition error — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize.go `finalizePrepareFailed`; session_terminal.go `failFinalizing`.
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 item 4's held `/finalize` rows take about 11 s each, because the margin uses the constant `watchdog.DefaultTickInterval` (5 s) however short the test's budget and tick are. Run them with `t.Parallel()` as `launch_bound_test.go` does. Do not add a Server option to shrink the margin, because the gateway never sets the watchdog's `TickInterval` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go `DefaultTickInterval`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/launch_bound_test.go.
WATCHOUT: `claimOrphanTimeout`'s windows (§5.2) stay accurate with the two-tick margin, because `prepareAtFinalize` materializes nothing on a concurrent-workspace slot and the combined path takes plain `launchContext`. Rework this if a slot path ever prepares at finalize — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize_handler.go comment above the `prepareAtFinalize` call.
USEFUL [non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]: its FACTs on the tick, the combined path, and the detached release all held on re-verification.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G3.1]
FACT: the CRD pattern `^https://[^/?#@]+([/?#]|$)` agrees with `net/url` on the rows item 12 pins: it refuses `https://user:tok@host/a.git`, `https://tok@host`, `https://`, and `https:///a`, and admits `https://host/a@b.git`, `https://host`, `https://host?x=1`, and `https://host#@x`, where `u.User` is nil. It refuses `HTTPS://host/a`, which `ValidateGitURL` admits: the scheme-case divergence the design chose. — EVIDENCE: a throwaway `regexp` plus `url.Parse` run over those strings
WATCHOUT: `URL` must keep `omitempty` like `Mode`, because the field pattern runs on any present value, so a Go client serializing `url: ""` on an inline entry fails the pattern. The existing `Mode` field already follows that form. — EVIDENCE: pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/runtime_types.go `SharedAsset.Mode`
DECISION: step 2 carries the extraction in a parenthetical and includes "keeps its empty-url check" in the staged text — BECAUSE the sentence is a comma list whose items would otherwise blur, and the design's invariant on `ReasonMissingRequired` has to reach the implementor — ALTERNATIVES: the design's "so `validateGitClone` calls it" clause verbatim (same meaning, harder to read inside the list).
WATCHOUT: the `ValidateGitURL` messages keep their gitClone wording ("gitClone url must not embed userinfo; use gitClone.auth.leaseScope ...") on the admin path, because gitClone behaviour must stay unchanged. It reads oddly on a `gitRepository` refusal and is not a defect. — EVIDENCE: pkg/workspaceplan/plan.go `validateGitClone`

### [non-spec.4.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: the owned-pool 409 applies on both tenant lifecycle entries, `handleDeleteTenant` and `handleForceDeleteTenant`, with no spec edit — BECAUSE §15.1 scopes force-delete to legal holds, the §15.1 Tenant dependents rule has no force-delete exception, and staged §6.4 rule 2 applies it to tenant-owned pools — ALTERNATIVES: a check inside `transitionToDisabling` (`handleDeleteTenant` does not call it, and it runs inside the tenant `Update` closure); a staged ordering clause beside `refuseSingleTenantDefaultDeletion` (item 31's with and without `acknowledgeHoldOverride` rows already fail a check placed on one path, and which 409 wins when a hold and a pool coexist is immaterial).
WATCHOUT: `handleForceDeleteTenant` has two `transitionToDisabling` call sites, one in the `!body.AcknowledgeHoldOverride` branch and one on the override path. A check inside only that branch leaves the override path open. Read pools without `IncludeDeleted`, because a soft-deleted owned pool must not block its tenant (item 32 deletes the pool and then the tenant). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/forcedelete.go `handleForceDeleteTenant`
WATCHOUT: do not widen the summary's tenant-deletion defect to unowned pools. §15.1 keeps pool records platform-global, so an unowned pool is under no tenant and the gap is sessions and credential pools only. §12.8 Phases 1 and 2 accept the request and drain sessions, so enforcing the whole §15.1 Tenant rule is a spec contradiction outside 0111. — EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md §15.1 **Deletion semantics for resources with dependents.**, spec/12_storage-architecture.md §12.8 **Tenant deletion lifecycle.**
WATCHOUT: item 31's `auth.leaseScope` row is the only one that fails a predicate comparing entries by `url` alone, because such a predicate treats a changed `url` as an add and rejects it. `auth.mode` is an enum of one value, so no `mode` row can be built. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 step 4
OPEN: item 31 has no row for removing `auth` from a private entry. Rule 2 says "changes a private entry's `url` or `auth`", and a private entry with no `auth` stops being private, so a later test-coverage lens should decide whether the removal is covered by a rule 2 row.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G5.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 item 39 at tier 8 is the one home of the §12.4 lineage failure matrix (entry path against fault), and item 36 lost its Redis-stopped sentence and its 12.4 annotation — BECAUSE test-coverage.md places a datastore-loss and recovery path at tier 8, and one matrix needs one home — ALTERNATIVES: stretching item 36 keeps a recovery path at tier 4; a distinct gRPC code for rule 7 cannot tell an empty record from a pod that never had a grant, and the classifier ignores the code outside the credential chain.
FACT: The earlier UNVERIFIED on acknowledged-write loss is settled: a `PERMISSION_DENIED` fetch on Resume is retried. `failResume` wraps through `slotBindError` with no `SetupCommandFailure` or `CredentialAssignmentError`, so `resumeFailureReason` returns `runtime_crash` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go `failResume`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go `resumeFailureReason`; credential_rejection_internal_test.go "a rejection outside a credential assignment is a crash".
FACT: A derived session that never checkpointed still takes the Resume checkpoint branch, because the derive copies the source's snapshot onto the derived row; only a session with no `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` is rebuilt from its plan — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go `copySnapshotRef` on the row; resume_rebind.go `resumeOnPod` nil-snapshot branch.
WATCHOUT: Item 39's resume row must use a session that has not checkpointed since its derive. On a no-store pod rule 6 removes `lenny.storeCommit`, so a resume from the session's own checkpoint makes no fetch and passes with no fault reaching it. Drive it through `Server.DriveResume`: `resumeFailureReason` runs in the driver, so `Binder.Resume` alone skips the assertion — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go `reportResumeFailure`.
WATCHOUT: Item 35's "With Redis stopped the Resume proceeds with no record." stays. It pins the CODE-9 step 2 `Grant` error rule at tier 4 on a fake adapter, which item 39 does not assert.
DECISION: Item 4's binder rows run through a site table over both `stageWorkspace` call sites, with a builder assertion on `exclusiveBindRequest` and `slotBindRequest` — BECAUSE a binder test sets the request fields itself and cannot see the builder drop a value, and a table states each case once — ALTERNATIVES: one slot-only row leaves the existing rows exclusive-only; item 15 at tier 4 costs a concurrent-pool stack for a field-carrying defect.
WATCHOUT: Both call sites compute `WorkspaceRoot` as `firstNonEmpty(req.ArchivePolicy.GetWorkspaceRoot(), slotlayout.SessionCurrentDir(...))`. Item 4's symlink rows leave the policy root empty, or they exercise a test-supplied root. The refusal reason is `ReasonPathEscapesRoot`, and item 3 owns reason assertions — EVIDENCE: bindprepare.go `allow`, slotbinder.go `allow`, pkg/upload/upload.go `ValidateSymlinkTarget`.
WATCHOUT: Item 4's limit row uses a tree under 512Mi. A tree over 512Mi is refused even with the limit dropped, so it discriminates nothing.
USEFUL [non-spec.4.fix-design-G5]: The design's resume reachability and classifier claims both held against the tree, so no redesign was needed.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: rule M2 constructs the Mirror in the `--agent-namespace` block of `buildPodLifecycle`, fatal on failure, and `--git-mirror-dir` keeps the chart path as its default — BECAUSE a Mirror then exists exactly when a cluster client does, so the handler needs no nil arm (rule P1 refuses first) and a broken chart volume fails at startup — ALTERNATIVES: an empty flag default with a second gate (adds a nil-Mirror arm to the handler and silently disables the cache on a misrendered chart); lazy root preparation with a sticky error (keeps a Mirror on gateways that cannot serve one and moves a volume fault to the first fill).
WATCHOUT: a "gateway exits with `--agent-namespace`" row that runs with no kubeconfig passes for the wrong reason, because `ctrl.GetConfig` fatals first. The row needs a cluster config and must assert the log line. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:153-157; refusal pattern with exec at tests/tier4_integration/holder_forward_startup_test.go:47.
WATCHOUT: the item 4 wiring test passes as nil == nil on wiring without `--agent-namespace`, now that M2 gates construction. It has to assert non-nil. EVIDENCE: wiringWithRedis in cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_holder_wiring_test.go:42 parses no agent namespace.
FACT: the one launch outside the chart that sets `--agent-namespace` is `adminCluster.gatewayOptions`, and two processes start from it, so each needs its own mirror directory. EVIDENCE: tests/tier4_integration/jwt_keyset_cross_process_test.go:114,340-341.
CORRECTS [Standing context: Open, "Mirror wiring test"]: TEST-1 item 4 already carries both the nil-Mirror row and the shared-Mirror wiring test, so the open item and the caller's carried directive are closed by existing text. The new gap is only the non-nil assertion above.
DECISION: delete the default gateway CPU limit (values.yaml, dev.yaml, e2e-values.yaml) instead of sizing it, and leave rule M7, its label, and the rules' scope line unchanged — BECAUSE the M3 and M4 limiters already bound the children, a quota adds a pause and no bound, and a formula limit has to be re-derived whenever an operator changes concurrency — ALTERNATIVES: limit = request + fetches + packs (10.25 cores at the defaults; it leaves the gateway only its 250m beside ten busy holders, below the 1 core it has today, and adds edits to M7, its label, the scope line, and all three values files); raising the request (reserves cores for a burst); `nice` on git children (needs a wrapper binary in the image and helps only on a saturated node).
DECISION: INFRA-1 phase (g) stays unchanged — BECAUSE the spike runs git with no CPU quota and the deployed container now has none, so the mismatch the finding names is gone by construction. A quota "equal to the mirror's share" would not bind anyway: W single-threaded packs never reach 10 cores.
FACT: go.mod is `go 1.25.0`, so the runtime's default GOMAXPROCS follows the cgroup CPU limit. Today's 1-core limit caps it, and deleting the limit lets it reach the node's logical CPUs. No spec, chart, or cmd/lenny-gateway text sets GOMAXPROCS. EVIDENCE: go.mod; lookup for GOMAXPROCS over spec, docs, and charts returned none.
WATCHOUT: git CPU counts toward the HPA's 70% utilization target against a 250m request, so warm fills scale the gateway out. This already holds at the 1-core limit (up to 400%), and deleting the limit raises the ceiling; maxReplicas still clamps it, and §10.1.6 keeps queue depth primary. Do not file it as new without showing harm the 400% case lacks. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:2925-2929; spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.1.6.
OPEN: on a node saturated to its requests, the gateway cgroup gets about its 250m weight and git children share it with the Go threads. Neither a limit nor its absence changes that. A later round may weigh `nice` or a higher request if a reviewer shows it on a realistic node.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: `/finalize` binds under `launchBound()+2*watchdog.DefaultTickInterval`, wrapping `prepareAtFinalize` alone, and `POST /v1/sessions/start` binds under plain `launchContext` — BECAUSE plain `launchContext` on `/finalize` expires within milliseconds of the budget while the watchdog fires on a 5 s tick, so the handler would nearly always win, `failFinalizing` would stamp no reason, and the client would get 503 instead of the 409 in docs/api/rest.md and §6.2's `FINALIZE_TIMEOUT` would stop appearing; the two ticks keep the watchdog first with no spec or docs edit, and the combined path has no row or watchdog, so it needs no margin — ALTERNATIVES: stamping `FINALIZE_TIMEOUT` and answering 409 in `finalizePrepareFailed` (changes `failFinalizing` and a response path for one race); rewriting M6 to say the request context (leaves the combined path unbounded); cancelling the prepare from the terminal write (a new registry); a bound inside `Binder.Prepare` (the binder does not know `launchBound`, and the resume path already has its own bound).
FACT: the gateway never sets `watchdog.Config.TickInterval`, so the sweep runs at `DefaultTickInterval` (5 s), and `launchBound()` and the watchdog's budget read the same flag — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:181,308; cmd/lenny-gateway/sessionsrv.go:376; cmd/lenny-gateway/controlserver.go:216; no `TickInterval` for the watchdog under cmd/.
FACT: `POST /v1/sessions/start` persists no row until after the bind, so the finalizing watchdog cannot end it, and `startOnPod(r.Context())` has only the client connection as a bound today — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_and_start.go:473, doc comment at :403-408.
FACT: `workspace.MaterializeWithPolicy` takes no context, and only `restoreSeed(ctx, ...)` carries the RPC context into it, so a fetcher injected through `ArchivePolicy` or an options struct has no deadline unless it is bound to the RPC's context — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/materialize.go:171; pkg/adapter/staging.go:314-315.
FACT: a binder failure past the deadline still releases: `reclaimOnFailure` reaches `failPhase`, which deletes the claim on `detachedReleaseContext`, and `failFinalizing` detaches with `WithoutCancel` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:53-62,119-121; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_terminal.go:157-158.
WATCHOUT: a late watchdog can still lose the race past the two ticks. The row then ends `failed` with no reason and the call answers `SESSION_CREATION_FAILED`, as any prepare failure near the budget does today; this is accepted. CODE-9's `LineageTTL = launchBound()` can lapse inside the margin, by which point the row has already failed, so it needs no change — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-9 step 1.
WATCHOUT: `launchContext`'s comment and `Options.MaxFinalizing`'s comment name only slot launches; the implementor widens them when wiring the combined path — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:295-301, sessionserver.go:1242-1249.
CORRECTS [non-spec.2.review-performance.1]: `/finalize` has no deadline in the tree; it holds only after this fix's **Bind deadline.** and CODE-5 step 5's context clause land.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: gate gitRepository urls in two places: the admin path calls `workspaceplan.ValidateGitURL` (extracted from `validateGitClone`), and the CRD path gets a field `Pattern` `^https://[^/?#@]+([/?#]|$)` on `URL`, replacing the CEL `^https://` clause — BECAUSE one regex closes https, empty host, and userinfo at the same length, and the pod flag is encoded from the CRD alone, so only an apiserver gate stops a token reaching `--shared-assets` — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's second CEL clause `!matches('^https://[^/?#]*@')` (two rules for one predicate, and a `matches` on an unbounded string under an unbounded list); running the Go check in the step 3 `mirror` (a `permanentError` there stops the registry row but not the pod flag).
DECISION: spec-changes.md:96 stays as staged, and the §14 omission goes in the summary defects list — BECAUSE the sentence stays true (the entry is literally under the check gitClone uses once it is shared), the spec loop converged, and the implementor builds the same thing either way — ALTERNATIVES: a "no userinfo" clause in SPEC-2 (reopens the converged spec loop for a rule whose natural home is §14 `gitClone.url` restrictions).
DECISION: scheme case stays divergent: the admin path accepts `HTTPS://` through `ValidateGitURL` and the CRD pattern refuses it — BECAUSE §14 already states both (case-insensitive at parse, `pattern` `^https://`), the divergence is stricter on the only path that reaches pods, and keeping `ValidateGitURL` unchanged keeps gitClone behaviour unchanged.
DECISION: a token in a url path or query is out of scope — BECAUSE no gate can identify one in a path, `validateGitClone` does not refuse a query either, and parity with gitClone is what the fix delivers.
FACT: the `validateGitClone` userinfo refusal has no test: no `pkg/workspaceplan` test uses a userinfo https url, so the extraction needs a Parse row in item 12. — EVIDENCE: pkg/workspaceplan/plan.go:778-787; grep of pkg/workspaceplan/*_test.go
FACT: `validateSharedAssets` runs on every admin write: create, update, and the bootstrap seed. The assignment at runtimes.go:1049 follows the check at :1198 through `validateUpdateRuntimeBody`. Step 2 therefore needs no new call site. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/runtimes.go:819, :1198, bootstrap.go:655
FACT: neither §14 nor §13 states the userinfo refusal, although the plan.go comment cites "§14 / §13.5". — EVIDENCE: lookup of `userinfo` over spec (Kubernetes admission hits only); grep of spec/13 and spec/14 for in-URL credential wording
WATCHOUT: item 12's prefix changes from "CRD CEL" to "CRD validation" because `url` is a field pattern now; the "not in CEL" sentence at the end of CODE-2 step 2 is about normalization and stays. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:38, :289

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G4.1]

DECISION: the force-delete refusal is a one-word-scale replace in CODE-8 step 4 plus a two-transition row in item 31 (with and without `acknowledgeHoldOverride`), with no ordering sentence — BECAUSE the row pins both `transitionToDisabling` sites in `handleForceDeleteTenant`, and which 409 wins when a hold and an owned pool coexist changes nothing — ALTERNATIVES: a staged ordering clause beside `refuseSingleTenantDefaultDeletion` (a reviewer aid, the row already fails a misplaced check); a §15.1 spec edit (not forced: §15.1 scopes force-delete to legal holds and the Tenant dependents row has no exception).
DECISION: the new defect bullet names sessions and credential pools only, and omits the finding's "unowned pools" — BECAUSE §15.1 and §4 keep pool records platform-global with no `tenant_id`, so an unowned pool is not "under the tenant"; `pool_tenant_access` is a visibility grant — ALTERNATIVES: copying the finding's wording, which files a defect the tree does not have. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:879, spec/04_system-components.md:174.
FACT: only two routes enter the deletion lifecycle. `tenantStateDisabler.SoftDisableTenant` (cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:48) also writes `disabling`, and it runs as Phase 1 of a job the runner rebuilt from a tenant already in `disabling` or `deleting`, so it is not a third entry and needs no check. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:25-33, :398.
FACT: §12.8 and §15.1 state different behaviors for running sessions on tenant deletion: §15.1 blocks, §12.8 Phases 1 and 2 accept and drain. The tree follows §12.8. The new summary bullet records it; 0111 does not resolve it. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1341, spec/12_storage-architecture.md (Tenant deletion lifecycle, Phases 1 and 2), pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenants.go:1697.
WATCHOUT: the step 4 check must read `r.pools.List` with the default `ListFilter`. `IncludeDeleted` would make a soft-deleted owned pool block its tenant forever, and item 32's pool-then-tenant order is the row that fails it. `r.pools` can be nil on a Router (routes.go:410 skips the pool routes), and existing tenant-delete tests build such a Router, so the check treats nil as no owned pool. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/poolstore/poolstore.go:931, pkg/gateway/runtime/poolstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:356, pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/routes.go:410.
FACT: the tenant-admin clause has no entry-identity rule, and `mergeSharedAssets` keys by `DestPath` while uniqueness keys by normalized url. A predicate comparing url sets sees a changed `url` as an add and rejects it, so the `url` row fails a count-based predicate and only the `auth.leaseScope` row fails a url-set predicate. That is why item 31 names the `auth.leaseScope` row as the discriminating one. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/runtimestore/merge.go:217, non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 step 2.
OPEN: item 31 has no row for a `globex` tenant-admin removing `auth` from a private entry, which is also a change to its `auth`. The Runtime clause of rule 2 catches it only when no private entry remains. Below this round's bar and outside G4's finding; a later test-coverage lens decides.
USEFUL [Standing context "Item 32 order"]: confirmed the block is relied on, and saved tracing the soft-delete path a second time.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G5.1]
FACT: a FetchGitPack failure during Resume, PERMISSION_DENIED included, is retried. `Binder.Resume` wraps `cl.Resume`'s error through `failResume` and `slotBindError`, neither of which adds a `SetupCommandFailure` or `CredentialAssignmentError`, so `resumeFailureReason` returns `runtime_crash` and `reportResumeFailure` takes the §6.2 retry branch. This closes the spec.5.review-reliability.1 UNVERIFIED; TEST-1 item 39 pins it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go failResume; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go resumeFailureReason.
WATCHOUT: a resume of a derived session fetches through the lineage pool only while the restored repository still carries `lenny.storeCommit`. Rule 6 removes the key on a no-store pod, so a resume from the session's own later checkpoint makes no fetch and a lineage-loss row there passes without exercising anything. The reachable case is a first pod lost before the session checkpoints, because `resumeOnPod` then restores `row.WorkspaceSnapshot`, the derived snapshot. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md §14 rule 6; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go resumeOnPod.
WATCHOUT: `Binder.Resume` alone never runs `resumeFailureReason`; a row that needs the retry classification drives `Server.DriveResume`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go.
WATCHOUT: a binder test sets the request fields itself, so it cannot see `slotBindRequest` dropping the CODE-5 step 1 values; the builders need their own assertion. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_request.go.
DECISION: the §12.4 failure row gets its own tier 8 item 39 as a pair table of entry path and fault, and item 36's outage sentence moves into it — BECAUSE test-coverage.md puts a datastore-loss recovery path at tier 8 and one table is the single home — ALTERNATIVES: a third extension of item 36 (the round 1 answer, wrong tier); keeping the item 36 sentence beside a tier 8 item (the same case stated twice).
DECISION: finding 2 runs item 4's binder rows on both call sites through a site table instead of a slot-only row — BECAUSE the existing refusal and nil-Mirror rows have the same blind spot, and the `restoreBindPaths` idiom already exists — ALTERNATIVES: a slot-only row, which leaves two parallel rows to drift.
USEFUL [Deferred D6, Mirror wiring test, First-bind restore test row]: all three are already staged, in items 8, 4, and 11, so the standing-context entries can retire.
OPEN: CODE-5 step 1 lists three carried values and still says "both call sites pass both values". The wording predates the owner field; G2 or a later round should correct it.
UNVERIFIED: `upload.ValidateSymlinkTarget` returns `ReasonPathEscapesRoot` for an escaping target while §14 rule 2 names `symlink`; whether CODE-3 step 4 maps it, and which reason item 3's row asserts. The item 4 site-table row names no reason for that reason.

FACT: the admin router has two routes into the tenant deletion lifecycle: DELETE /v1/admin/tenants/{id} (`handleDeleteTenant`) and POST .../force-delete (`handleForceDeleteTenant`). §15.1 scopes force-delete to bypassing legal holds only, and neither handler checks dependents today (no RESOURCE_HAS_DEPENDENTS in tenants.go or forcedelete.go). FACT: measured on git 2.50.1 against github.com/git/git.git: one ls-remote ran 0.37 s, `/usr/bin/time` showed 10.9 MiB maxrss (one child only), and the summed git process RSS sample peaked near 18.9 MiB. RSS double-counts shared text, so the cgroup charge per pair is somewhat lower. An ls-remote of linux.git did not finish inside a 40 s timeout here. This resolves the UNVERIFIED at non-spec.1.review-performance.2 on per-process RSS.

### [redesign.1.apply]
FACT: all 112 edits of `redesign-1` were already in the staged files when this applier started (file times 10:16, one scripted write). A per-edit check found every `new` text present and every replaced anchor gone, so nothing was reapplied; a dry rerun would have duplicated the insert-only edits. This pass did the reconciliation, the sweep, and the related-passages lookup, and this block is the record.
DECISION: three areas were redesigned. The gateway mirror resource envelope (CODE-3 step 7 rules M2, M3, M6, M7, M9, M10, and M11, step 9) answers the non-spec loop's unconverged findings that git shares the gateway's cgroup with no per-process bound. Bind deadlines moved into the new §7.1 **Bind bounds.** (SPEC-12, CODE-5, CODE-9 `finalizeBound`), because "every bind runs on a context with a deadline" was false of `/finalize` and `POST /v1/sessions/start`. Repository url admission moved into numbered §14 **`gitClone.url` restrictions.** rules (SPEC-3 Edit (g), CODE-2 step 6). Summary Decisions 27, 28, and 29 carry the reasons.
DECISION: deleted with this redesign: the measured `P_fetch` and `P_slot` terms of rule M7, the rule M2 bound in the largest single fetch, the repeated `haves` field, `core.deltaBaseCacheSize`, the CODE-3 step 9 core inequality over the CPU request, the two summary defects on the cold clone's bounds and on the userinfo refusal, and the bind-deadline clauses of TEST-1 item 4, which item 40 replaces.
DECISION: reconciliation added what the edit list left out: `GIT_CLONE_REF_RESOLVE_TRANSIENT` in S6 and in the SPEC-3 and SPEC-6 index lines, the probe-limit cause in the spec-changes overview, `pkg/workspaceplan` in the CODE-3 index, `crds_test.go` in CODE-2 Targets, `stores.go`, `main.go`, and `archive_test.go` in Targets or Files touched, the rule set named in TEST-1 item 12's "fails rule 1" row, and a non-goal reworded because 0111 now does limit the cold path.
FACT: `redesign-1` E22, E22a, E22b, and SPEC-12 Edits (b) and (c) quote text that matches spec/ verbatim. The related-passages lookup found no spec or docs site the staged edits leave wrong. spec/18 §18.13 "rejected at schema validation" still reads true of `workspaceplan.Parse`. No spec or docs page states a gateway CPU limit or `GOMAXPROCS`.
WATCHOUT: do not restore a measured sum of summed peaks in rule M7. It holds only for the spike's repository, so a larger repository can reach the cgroup limit and end every session on the replica; the sum of ceilings under rule M9's `ulimit -d` cannot be reached. Keep this trap.
WATCHOUT: do not put `GOMEMLIMIT` or a git sidecar in place of rules M9 and M10, and do not use `shareProcessNamespace`: Decision 27 rejects the first and the third, and a sidecar needs a second image, a transport, and its own sizing.
WATCHOUT: do not set `/finalize` to a plain `launchContext`, and do not rely on the watchdog alone. The plain bound expires with the watchdog's budget, so the handler usually wins and answers 503 with no reason; the watchdog cancels no context, so a bind queued on a mirror slot would run for a failed session. Decision 28 states both.
WATCHOUT: do not restore `HTTPS://` admission or the "scheme case stays divergent" DECISION: git resolves its helper from the exact spelling, so no `HTTPS://` source worked. Do not add a third regex to the published schema, and keep the CRD pattern as the second gate because `createPod` reads the CRD alone. `gitClone.url` rule numbers collide with the cache-served §14 rules, so name the rule set at every citation.
OPEN: OD-1, cold clone admission. Staged default: a cold clone takes a `maxConcurrentFetches` slot, so with the default `2` it can queue behind warm refills and fail at the §7.1 bound. Alternative: a separate `maxConcurrentClones` limiter, at the cost of one chart value and one rule M7 term.
OPEN: OD-2, size of the rule M7 sum. Staged default: a sum of ceilings, about 20 GiB plus 18 x S at D = 1 GiB, far above the `256Mi` request, so node pressure can evict the pod first. Alternatives: the measured sum (restores the OOM exposure), or lower `maxConcurrentPacks` or a higher request once INFRA-1 records D.

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: applied the G1 design as written: rule 5 names the `git rev-list --objects` listing with `lenny.baseCommit` and that commit's tree excluded, rule 6 runs "the rule 5 listing", row "A checkpoint restores onto a pod..." loses "or index", CODE-6 step 1 gains `<lenny.baseCommit>^{tree}`, step 2.5 cites step 1, and TEST-1 item 10 gains the pre-pin revert and no-commit cases. Row "A session commit reuses a blob..." and summary Decision 10 stay unedited — BECAUSE the listing copies a blob reused from before the pin, so rule 5's reachability wording was the wrong text — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's narrowing of the row plus an extended accepted failure (it states a failure the command does not produce).
CORRECTS [spec.6.review-reliability.1]: the Standing context Open line "**Revert example:**" reads the wrong way round. Rule 5 was wrong and the example was right, and the rule 5 correction closes it, so the compaction pass should retire the line.
FACT: a throwaway alternates-backed repository on git 2.50.1 reproduces every row of this fix through a capture and a restore with a true shallow pack (base commit plus its tree closure, `shallow`=base). A revert of the pinned change copies the earlier blob, and the listing and `git fsck --connectivity-only` pass. A session with no commit copies nothing. A pre-pin blob staged only in the index is copied and restores. An amend, and an amend followed by a reset to base, fail the listing with "fatal: revision walk setup failed" — EVIDENCE: local reproduction under /tmp, removed afterwards.
WATCHOUT: a reproduction of the amend failure needs a pack with base's commit and tree closure only. `git pack-objects --revs` on base brings the parent commit, the listing then returns rc=0, and the case looks like it passes. A run in this round fell into this before it was corrected — EVIDENCE: first reproduction attempt, spec.1.fix-G1 session.
WATCHOUT: `shallow`=base stops the exclusion at base, so a walk from an amended commit no longer treats the parent as excluded and goes on to read it. The same listing that excludes the parent on an unrestored slot lists it on a restored one, which is why the amend fails only at restore — EVIDENCE: same reproduction, listing with `shallow` written returned the parent commit, its tree, and its blob.
USEFUL [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]: the design's listing claims held on independent reproduction, and its note that the amend and reset case had not been rerun is now closed by the FACT above.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: applied the G2 design: SPEC-3 Edit (i) puts restore completion inside §10.1.7 step 4 on a partial-manifest resume, rule 6 gained one pointer sentence, and one Edge row qualifies the unqualified resume-failure rows — BECAUSE step 4's staging delete and "no partially-extracted files" sentence hold only before the rename, and the closing "any of the non-terminal failure modes in step 4" picks the new failure up with no third fallback statement — ALTERNATIVES: as listed in spec.1.fix-design-G2.1.
DECISION: CODE-6 step 4 carries the design's first two sentences and omits "Take the tree root as a parameter, so that fix can run the completion on the staged tree." — BECAUSE the non-spec grant bars authoring a code change a spec edit implies, while the two kept sentences repair CODE-6 step 2.5's unconditional `FailedPrecondition`, which Edit (i) would otherwise contradict on a partial resume. The omitted sentence changes nothing, because steps 2 and 3 already run completion on two different trees (the slot and the build tree) and the helper opens from "the root of the tree that step 2.1's walk covers" — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the sentence as the design wrote it (an unreviewed code instruction under a spec lens).

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the row 27 / rule 5 contradiction by correcting rule 5 toward what the CODE-6 listing does, naming the `git rev-list --objects` listing with `lenny.baseCommit` and that commit's tree excluded; rule 6 repeats that listing; row 27, summary Decision 10, and the non-goal "Accepting the corrupt-restore case" stay as they are — BECAUSE git excludes the trees of boundary commits and not all history, so a blob a session commit reverts to from before the pin IS listed and copied, and a revert restores correctly — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's narrowing of row 27 plus an extended row 30 (states a failure the listing does not produce, so its test row fails against CODE-6, and accepts a failure on an ordinary `git revert`); a set-exact exclusion of all ancestry (needs the whole-history walk summary Non-goals rejects).
DECISION: rule 5's exclusion gains baseCommit's tree (`--not <base> <base>^{tree}`) — BECAUSE with no session commit there is no boundary commit, so no tree is marked uninteresting and `--indexed-objects` lists the whole index; naming the listing in rule 5 without the tree exclusion would specify copying every blob of the tree into every checkpoint, which the old set wording forbade — ALTERNATIVES: filing it separately (the fix would write the regression into rule 5).
FACT: `git rev-list --objects C --not C^` lists a blob that C's parent tree lacks and an older ancestor holds. Two real reverts in this repo's history reproduce it: 78b8fc4df (pkg/gateway/billingstore/billingstore.go, blob 46597409) and ce40ac2a5 (pkg/gateway/policy/quotaevaluator.go, blob 2c536ba7), git 2.50.1 — EVIDENCE: read-only rev-list runs, no files created.
FACT: `git rev-list --objects --indexed-objects HEAD --not HEAD` listed 10318 objects on an 8124-file tree; adding `HEAD^{tree}` to the negative side left 741, all index resolve-undo and cache-tree residue of a merge in that worktree (no index blob differs from HEAD's tree). `--not --end-of-options <sha> <sha>^{tree}` parses and gives the same count — EVIDENCE: read-only runs in draft-0111.
WATCHOUT: "walk" already names the filesystem traversal in the §13.4 exception and CODE-6 step 2.1, so rule 5 and rule 6 say "listing" for the rev-list — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-4 exception "the workspace tree that rule 5 or 6 operates on", non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 step 2.1.
WATCHOUT: the tree exclusion removes the accidental index-blob check from rule 6 on a session with no commit. That is not a lost guard: with a session commit the base tree was already excluded, and the fetch is the guard for baseCommit's closure — EVIDENCE: review-log-archive.md:89.
WATCHOUT: this fix does not reopen the amend/reset class. It copies no ancestor commit or ancestor tree closure, row 30's failure stays (`revision walk setup failed` on the missing parent, review-log-archive.md:305), and Standing context "Do not graft a shallow frontier" still holds — EVIDENCE: review-log-archive.md:283.
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]: the archived DECISION lists "index blobs from older trees" among what needs its own enumeration. `--indexed-objects` already lists an index blob outside the excluded trees, so such a blob is copied and only refs or reflogs reach the accepted failure; row 30 loses "or index".
USEFUL [spec.6.review-reliability.1]: the archived WATCHOUT framed this as "only the example is wrong"; it reasoned from rule 5's set wording and did not run the listing, so the example was right and the rule was wrong.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]

DECISION: a partial-manifest resume runs §14 rule 6 on the staged tree before the §10.1 step 4 rename, and a rule 6 failure joins the step 4 non-terminal failures (SPEC-3 Edit (i), plus one pointer sentence in rule 6) — BECAUSE step 4's staging delete and its "no partially-extracted files" guarantee only hold before the rename, and step 4 already routes every listed failure to the full-checkpoint fallback, so the closing "any of the non-terminal failure modes in step 4" picks the new failure up with no third fallback statement — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's rule-6-only sentence (leaves completion after the rename, so a failure leaves partial files in `current` and §10.1's closed list disagrees); a rollback of `current` after the rename (a second cleanup path that nearly duplicates the staging delete); skipping a broken repository on a partial resume (needs a Resume field against summary Decision 11, and restores a repository with missing objects); fallback only on the next attempt (costs a retry, parks the session on an exhausted budget, which is the finding).

DECISION: 0111 builds no partial-manifest branch in CODE-6, and the unbuilt failure path is filed by extending F-10.1.37 in the summary defects list — BECAUSE no partial resume reaches rule 6 in the tree (see FACT below), and building the staged pipeline, the failed-Resume fallback, and failure-path cleanup is the open reassembly work rather than git-store work.

FACT: the §10.1 reassembly failure path is unbuilt. `workspace.ExtractTree` (pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go) errors on an end-truncated stream (F-10.1.37, open), extraction goes straight to the slot roots with no `current.partial` (pkg/adapter/resume.go `restoreChunks`), `resolveResumeChunks` falls back only on gateway-side `ErrReassemblyContiguity` and `ErrBelowRecoveryThreshold` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_checkpoint.go), an adapter-side Resume error classifies as `runtime_crash` (resume_driver.go `resumeFailureReason`), and `CleanupAfterResume` runs only from `resumeTail.events()` after a landed commit, so a failed attempt reselects the same partial row. TEST-GAPS T-10.1.6 records the pipeline as untested. EVIDENCE: BUILD-GAPS.md F-10.1.37; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go `resumeTail.events`.

WATCHOUT: a truncation that cuts `.git` before `config` leaves a `.git` with no `lenny.gitStoreUrl`, so rule 6 skips it and the session resumes as `partial_workspace` with no usable repository there. That is ordinary §10.1 member loss rather than a repository with a missing object, because git refuses a `.git` without config and objects. Do not re-file it against rule 6. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.1.7 step 3 ("treats everything after it as lost"); filepath.Walk order in pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go.

WATCHOUT: the §10.1 fallback restore is itself a full-manifest resume, so its own completion failure is a plain resume failure under rule 6. The §12.4 lineage row therefore stays true on a partial resume: a Redis outage fails the fallback restore too. EVIDENCE: SPEC-10 Edit (c).

WATCHOUT: §28.8 and §29.6 list reassembly failures without claiming the list is closed, and "renamed only once end truncation is the sole observed error" remains a true necessary condition, so neither needs an edit. §29 traces are already on the do-not-re-file list. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:2094; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:1189.

OPEN: §10.1 does not state what a fallback does when no full checkpoint exists (a partial-only session). `resolveFullCheckpointFallback` returns nil and restores nothing. Pre-existing and independent of rule 6; whoever closes F-10.1.37 should settle it.

### [spec.2.review-fresh.1]
FACT: The staged rule 5 listing behaves as rules 5 and 6 and the edge rows state on git 2.50.1: no session commit lists nothing, a revert child of baseCommit lists the commit, tree, and earlier blob, and a shallow restore whose refs reach an ancestor of baseCommit exits 128. EVIDENCE: scratch repositories run against `git rev-list --objects --all --reflog --indexed-objects --not <base> <base>^{tree}`.
WATCHOUT: When a commit's parent is an ancestor of baseCommit (amend or reset), rev-list treats that ancestor as an edge and omits its tree, so a reflogged revert to that tree is not copied. This is consistent with rule 5's closing sentence, and a shallow restore fails closed on the missing ancestor, so a test that expects the copy in the amend-plus-revert case would be wrong. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md rule 5, scratch run listing only the two commits after an amend.

### [spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: `git rev-list --objects --all --reflog --indexed-objects --not <B> <B>^{tree}` was run on git 2.50.1: no session commit lists nothing; a staged or committed revert lists the earlier blob; `--end-of-options` after `--not` behaves the same. An amend's parent edge marks that parent's tree uninteresting, so its objects are not copied; restore then relies on the store or fails closed, which the edge row on a different or absent store already states. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 rule 5
FACT: 0112 SPEC-7 (k) inserts after "remote-host timeout)." and SPEC-6 Edit (c) ends on that period, so the two compose when 0112 lands first. EVIDENCE: proposals/0112*/*.spec-changes.md:360

### [spec.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The materialization bound (§14 rule 2) gains one term, the commit object's size, taken from CODE-3 step 4's existing `git rev-list --objects --no-walk <sha>` | `cat-file --batch-check` listing — BECAUSE SPEC-5's shallow pack holds `want`'s commit object, git puts no limit on commit size, and rule 2 guarantees that a passing source stays inside `workspaceSizeLimitBytes` — ALTERNATIVES: a separate `git cat-file -s` (redundant process); a fixed allowance (commit size is unbounded); adding the commit to the §4.7.1 comparison or the tree-bytes memo (a delta's commit bytes already count on its side, and it would change a selection rule and every pack row); a new refusal reason (the `workspace_size_limit` refusal covers it); a new test row (the boundary rows of TEST-1 item 3 discriminate once their expected bound counts the commit).
FACT: The object listing prints the commit first and with an empty `%(rest)`, for example `commit 116 `, then the root tree with an empty rest, on git 2.50.1 — EVIDENCE: run locally against a throwaway repository; CODE-3 step 5 pipes the same listing into `pack-objects`, so it is the shallow pack's object set.
WATCHOUT: The commit-object term is in the bound and stays out of the tree-bytes memo and the "blobs and tree objects" comparison. The two sums diverge on purpose, so the verdict memo's bound and the tree-bytes memo are not interchangeable — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5 `FetchGitPack` row; non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 4 memo bullet and step 7 rule M5.
UNVERIFIED: Whether the bound's per-blob and per-entry overhead terms cover the rest of the rule 3 slot repository (the pack index, `index`, `config`, `shallow`, and HEAD). This fix did not examine them. A mechanism lens should check.

"A request that carries no `leaseScope`" would read as covering a `gitClone` source with no `auth`, which has no leaseScope and never binds. They also name no HTTP status, because an MCP tool error has none, and they never name `lenny/vcs_token`, because the spec calls it the credential helper. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-8 Edit (f); §4.9 **`hostPatterns`** rule 4 in Edit (b).

It is filed in the summary defects list. 0112 CODE-6 routes `NOT_FOUND`, `DENIED`, and a parse error into that sentinel. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools.go `vcsTokenError`; 0112 non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 1.

CORRECTS [Settled "0112 and 0111 are disjoint"]: SPEC-8 (f) now edits both `GIT_CLONE_AUTH_UNSUPPORTED_HOST` and `GIT_CLONE_AUTH_HOST_AMBIGUOUS`. The disjointness with 0112 SPEC-7 (k) still holds; only the row name in that line is stale.

### [spec.3.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: the §15.1 `UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` row (SPEC-6 Edit (a)) is the one home of "a rule 2 refusal is not counted in `lenny_upload_extraction_aborted_total`"; CODE-3 rule M4 is the mechanism and CODE-5 step 3 lost its partial copy — BECAUSE `recordExtractionAbort` is called only from `gatedExtract` and the CODE-5 step 2 `archiveDir` verdict, and ValidateTree runs outside both — ALTERNATIVES: propagating the sentence into CODE-5 step 3 (a third statement of one rule); carving §13.4 or §16.1 (SPEC-4 Edit (b) already places cache-served sources outside the upload pipeline).
WATCHOUT: do not write "a `gitClone` refusal is not counted". An ineligible source's `CloneArchive` `archiveDir` verdict is counted through CODE-5 step 2; the exclusion is scoped to rule 2, which applies only to cache-served sources. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go `gatedExtract`, `recordExtractionAbort`; non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 step 2.
WATCHOUT: counting rule 2 path and symlink refusals with a direct `recordExtractionAbort` call from the binder lost: it reverses rule M4, leaves `workspace_size_limit` as a special case, and records a tree walk as an archive-extraction abort although no archive is unpacked. TEST-1 item 4 now drives `symlink` too. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go `recordExtractionAbort`.
CORRECTS [Open "UNVERIFIED extraction-aborted counter"]: closed; a cache-served rule 2 path or symlink refusal does not increment the counter, and the §15.1 row now says so. Retire the Open line at the next compaction.

### [spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: add the commit object to §14 rule 2's materialization bound as one term, and take its size from CODE-3 step 4's existing object listing — BECAUSE the shallow pack holds `want`'s commit and git puts no limit on commit size, and the listing already prints that size — ALTERNATIVES: a new `git cat-file -s <sha>` call (redundant process); a fixed allowance for the commit (commit size is unbounded); a dedicated large-message refusal reason (the bound's `workspace_size_limit` already covers it).
FACT: `git rev-list --objects --no-walk <sha> | git cat-file --batch-check='%(objecttype) %(objectsize) %(rest)'` prints the commit first, as `commit <size>` with an empty rest, then the root tree. ls-tree supplies neither size, but no new git call is needed — EVIDENCE: run on the draft-0111 tree with git 2.50.1; non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 5 pipes the same listing into pack-objects.
WATCHOUT: the same listing feeds two sums. The bound includes the commit line, and the tree-bytes memo excludes it, because §4.7.1 compares a delta against "the blobs and tree objects of `want`'s tree". Sum by `objecttype` — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 4 memo sub-bullet; spec-changes.md SPEC-5 `FetchGitPack` row.
UNVERIFIED: rule 2's "does not exceed" is absolute, yet the bound has no term for the slot repository's constant files (pack header and trailer, idx fanout, config, HEAD, shallow) or for zlib expansion of incompressible blobs, which the 128-byte per-entry allowance does not scale with. The next spec-round mechanism lens should check this; it is outside G1.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `GitRef` carries an OpenAPI field pattern `^(.{1,39}|.{41,}|.*[^0-9a-f].*)$` in CODE-2 step 4, and TEST-1 item 12 gains the empty, 39-hex, 41-hex, and uppercase rows — BECAUSE the design chose it: a pattern does not count against the CEL cost budget and needs no bound — ALTERNATIVES: `MaxLength=255` plus `MaxItems=64` adds a list limit the spec does not state; a size-guarded `matches` costs the same estimate; a `permanentError` in `mirror` leaves the pod flag set; `not: {pattern}` has no controller-gen marker.
FACT: the pattern was run under Go `regexp`, closing the design's "not run under Go regexp" caveat: it refuses 40-hex, 40 digits, and empty, and admits 39-hex, 41-hex, uppercase 40, 39-hex plus `g`, `main`, `v1.2.3`, and 40-hex plus a trailing newline. A string with two or more newlines is refused, which is harmless because no git ref contains one. — EVIDENCE: throwaway `go run` in /tmp, removed
FACT: `SharedAsset.Mode` is the precedent for a pattern on an optional `omitempty` string, so an inline entry with an unset `GitRef` serializes no `gitRef` key and the pattern does not fire on it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/apis/lenny/v1alpha1/runtime_types.go `Mode` in `SharedAsset`
UNVERIFIED: the remaining `has` and `type` per-entry rules on the unbounded `sharedAssets` list were not applied to an API server here either; the design's arithmetic is the only evidence. Item 12's CRD rows fail at once on envtest if they are refused. The implementor checks it.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G2.1, non-spec.2.fix-G4.1]: Standing context **gitRef is a branch or tag:** still reads "CODE-2 plus a per-entry CEL rule". True text is "CODE-2 plus a CRD field pattern". This fixer left review-log.md untouched (the design's fallback): the task sends entries only to this shard, and parallel fixers were writing the log during this round. Compaction applies it.
CORRECTS [non-spec.4.fix-design-G3.1]: the UNVERIFIED at review-log.md "CODE-2 step 4's remaining per-entry CEL rule" is settled: the rule was measured as refused and is removed.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: replace the CODE-2 step 4 `gitRef` CEL `matches` rule with a field `Pattern` on `GitRef`, `^(.{1,39}|.{41,}|.*[^0-9a-f].*)$`, and add no `MaxLength` or `MaxItems` — BECAUSE OpenAPI `pattern` is outside the CEL cost budget, it is the same move non-spec.4.fix-design-G3.1 made for `url`, and it adds no limit the spec does not state — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's `MaxLength=255` plus `MaxItems=64` (admitted when measured, but it imposes a new 64-entry limit on existing `inline` Runtimes that §5.1 and §6.4 do not state, and the admin path would diverge unless a Go bound and a spec edit follow); dropping the CRD check and refusing in `mirror` (a `permanentError` stops the registry row and not the pod flag); OpenAPI `not` (controller-gen has no marker for it).
FACT: the cost arithmetic behind the finding's measurement: the apiserver counts `maxLength` as 4 bytes per rune and an enum string at its longest value, so `self.type == '...'` stays cheap. Cardinality of the unbounded `sharedAssets` list derived from the measured 10.1x factor is about 240k items, so a per-entry rule fits the 10M per-rule limit while it costs under about 40 units per item. `has(self.x)` costs 2 (cel-go checker/cost.go `costSelect`). — EVIDENCE: finding's envtest measurement; ~/go/pkg/mod/github.com/google/cel-go@v0.28.0/checker/cost.go:502
UNVERIFIED: the remaining `has` and `type` rules on the unbounded list were not applied to an apiserver in this pass, because this task forbids creating a scratch test; the arithmetic above is the only evidence. Item 12's CRD rows run on envtest, which installs charts/lenny/crds (tests/testinfra/envtest/envtest.go:121), so a refusal fails them at once. Implementor and the next review round check it.
WATCHOUT: `.{1,39}` is deliberate. `has(self.gitRef)` admits `gitRef: ""`, and the admin path refuses it, so `.{0,39}` would leave the empty ref open on the only path that reaches pods. The pattern was checked in Python `re` over 40-hex, 40-digit, 39, 41, uppercase 40, 39-hex plus `G`, `main`, `v1.2.3`, and empty; it was not run under Go regexp. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 step 2
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G2.1, non-spec.2.fix-G4.1]: Standing context "gitRef is a branch or tag" says "CODE-2 plus a per-entry CEL rule"; the CRD gate is now a field `Pattern` on `GitRef`.
CORRECTS [non-spec.4.fix-design-G3.1]: its UNVERIFIED on the `gitRef` `matches` rule is settled by the finding's measurement, and the rule is removed.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: the registered url reaches the mirror as a `remote` string parameter on `Ensure`, `ResolveRef`, and `Pack`, and `MirrorKey` stays `{Owner, URL}` — BECAUSE `MirrorKey` is the identity of every lock, memo, and directory hash (M1), and a field "excluded from identity" in a Go struct compared by value is a trap — ALTERNATIVES: `MirrorKey.Remote` (reviewer's suggestion; needs a custom identity method at every map site); `gitref.Remote{URL, Credential}` replacing `cred` (new type with no gain over `Clone`'s url-plus-credential); registration rejecting a `.git` url or dropping `.git` from §6.4 normalization (removes a working spelling or breaks eligibility across spellings).
DECISION: no spec edit for this finding — BECAUSE §6.4 confines normalization to comparison and rule 4 uses it only for the key, so the spec never tells the gateway to dial the normalized form; which url the fetch dials is a CODE-3 implementation rule with M1 as its home — ALTERNATIVES: a sentence in §6.4 rule 4, which no reader acts on beyond what "an entry names an HTTPS repository `url`" already says.
DECISION: step 3's fetch merge is per commit and `remote`, so a broken spelling on one Runtime cannot fail a concurrent fetch for another Runtime's spelling of the same key — BECAUSE fetches for one key already run one at a time, so the second fetch runs after the first and is a no-op when the first succeeded.
WATCHOUT: `Mirror.CloneArchive` also dials git, with the source's url verbatim, for an ineligible source, so a rule that "every network operation of `Mirror` dials `remote`" is false. M1 names `Ensure` and `ResolveRef` only; `Pack` reaches the network only through its `Ensure` calls. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 Binder step 2 ('`extractGitCloneSource` calls `CloneArchive` on `Binder.Mirror`'); pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/archiveextract.go:142
WATCHOUT: the request's url and the source's url are both unsafe substitutes for `remote`. Restore completion sends the normalized `lenny.gitStoreUrl`, and a `gitClone` source carries the client's spelling. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md §14 rule 3 ('records the normalized `url` as `lenny.gitStoreUrl`')
FACT: `authHeaderScope` keeps only scheme and lowercased host, so swapping the dial url between spellings of one normalized url never changes the credential's scope. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/cred.go:59
FACT: a Runtime's resolved `sharedAssets` hold at most one entry per normalized url, so the matched entry and its `remote` are unique per Runtime; two spellings of one key arise only across Runtimes. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:77

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 step 2.4 keeps the Resume's `FailedPrecondition` scoped to a Resume, step 3 stays the one home of the first-bind answer, and step 2.5 is reworded the same way — BECAUSE step 2.4's sentence covered the step 3 path while giving a code step 3 contradicts; restoreSeed wraps every error in errRestore, which FinalizeWorkspace answers Unavailable — ALTERNATIVES: FailedPrecondition on both paths (breaks the existing TestFinalizeWorkspaceRestoreFailures family and §7.1 "every restore failure" Unavailable); a new outcome table for completion failures (hair).
WATCHOUT: item 8's restore-completion rows for a FIFO or an escaping symlink cannot run through restore chunks, because ExtractTree refuses both first, so the row would pass without reaching the helper. Build the tree on disk — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go:200-205; pkg/adapter/staging_restore_test.go case "relative symlink leaving the build tree".
WATCHOUT: restoreSeed discards ExtractTree's byte count (`_, err := workspace.ExtractTree`), so step 2.4's "restored bytes" on the step 3 path needs that count kept. Item 11's first-bind over-limit rows use a pack that fits alone so a dropped count fails them — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/staging.go:405-422.
FACT: FinalizeWorkspaceRequest carries no limit field, and Resume carries `workspace_size_limit_bytes`, so the first-bind limit can only come from Server.WorkspaceSizeLimitBytes, which is 0 when the template leaves it unset — EVIDENCE: schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1432-1438; pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:573-575.
OPEN: CODE-6 bullet (3) reads `lenny.*` from "a copy of the repository's `config`", and step 2.1's walk reads config, but bullet (2)'s regular-files-only clause lists HEAD, packed-refs, refs/, logs/, index, shallow, and worktrees/ and not config. A FIFO or symlink at `config` is not stated to fail. Not closed by G3; a later round should decide whether bullet (3) copies as bullet (2) does, and add `config` to item 8 if so.
OPEN: CODE-6 names no Resume status for a FetchGitPack error or a helper failure in restore completion (item 11 only says the resume fails; existing extraction failures answer Internal, resume.go restoreChunks branch). Not closed by G3.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 bullet (3) reads the live `config` through a non-blocking `fstat`-typed descriptor, and a `config` that is absent, a symlink, non-regular, or malformed counts as no `lenny.*` keys — BECAUSE §14 rules 5 and 6 select on "whose config carries `lenny.gitStoreUrl`" and an agent can already drop the key from a regular file, so no spec edit is needed — ALTERNATIVES: rejected as listed in non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1 (add `config` to bullet (2), fail the operation, read-path copy, `Lstat` first, parse in Go).
WATCHOUT: do not add `config` to CODE-6 bullet (2)'s copied list, the finding's suggested fix. It lands at `$GIT_DIR/config`, where `core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`, and `include.path` take effect, which falsifies staged §13.4 and item 8's marker row; and the copy is all-or-nothing, so a `reftable/` repository with no keys would fail the capture. Rule 6's rewrite copy is named `config.edit` for the same reason, because its `rev-list` runs after the rewrite — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md §13.4 exception sentence "no configuration, hook, or include that the agent wrote takes effect".
WATCHOUT: the race rows in TEST-1 item 8 drive the helper, never a whole capture, because `archiveRoot` and `Archive` have their own lstat-then-`os.Open` window under `s.ops`, now filed in the summary defects list — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go `archiveRoot`; pkg/adapter/workspace/archive.go `Archive`; pkg/adapter/checkpointchunk.go `readChunk`.
WATCHOUT: treat only git exit 128 as malformed. Exit 1 needs no special case (no match), and a failed exec, a signal, or a context cancel must fail the operation, or a transient error silently skips a capture copy — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 bullet (3).
OPEN: a FIFO under `objects/` of a repository that carries `lenny.gitStoreUrl` stalls the op lock for up to the checkpoint deadline per attempt, in a git child the context kills. Left out by the design; a later round decides whether that bounded stall is acceptable.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1 WATCHOUT, review-log "not filed" on a symlinked or FIFO `config`]: the effect is not self-harm for a FIFO. A blocking in-process open runs under `s.ops`, which blocks co-tenants on a concurrent-workspace pod.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1 OPEN on bullet (3) `config`]: it named this gap and where to look.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 item 11 cites item 6's shallow-answer and failed-`index-pack` rows, run through restore completion, and adds only `baseCommit`, the `lenny.storeCommit` removal, and the failed second pack — BECAUSE two earlier rounds each added one prose twin of an item 6 row and the next round filed the next missing twin — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's verbatim clause beside the shallow twin (a third twin); stating the retry in CODE-5 step 6 (a third statement of §14 rule 3).
WATCHOUT: do not add a new item 6 pack-answer expectation to item 11 as prose, and do not assert a gRPC code for a second-pack failure on a Resume, because CODE-6 states `FailedPrecondition` only for the over-limit refusal and the missing object. ### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: a `config` that is absent, a symlink, non-regular by `fstat`, or malformed (git exit 128) carries no `lenny.*` keys, so the walk leaves that `.git` alone and no spec edit is needed — BECAUSE §14 rules 5 and 6 select on "whose config carries `lenny.gitStoreUrl`", and an agent can already drop that key from a regular `config`, so a skip hands it nothing new, and failing the capture would widen the failure scope to repositories the cache never touched — ALTERNATIVES: fail the operation on a non-regular `config` (needs a new §13.4 rejection and fails an agent's own repo); add `config` to bullet (2)'s copy (lands at `$GIT_DIR/config`, and every agent `.git` pays the all-or-nothing copy, including a `reftable/` failure); a separate scratch per walked `.git` (a mkdir and removal per `.git` per capture, twice).
DECISION: the read passes the `config` descriptor to git as `/proc/self/fd/N`, the pattern bullet (2) already uses for objects, with no copy on the read path — BECAUSE a regular inode cannot become a FIFO, so the fstat verdict holds for git's reopen, and no destination question exists. Only the rule 6 rewrite copies, to `config.edit` in the per-operation git directory, a name git never reads, removed with that directory.
FACT: `git config --file /proc/self/fd/3 --no-includes --get-regexp '^lenny\.'` works on git 2.50.1: exit 0 with keys, 1 on no match or empty file, 128 on a malformed file. An `O_NONBLOCK` read-only open of a FIFO returns at once and `fstat` reports S_ISFIFO — EVIDENCE: local prototype, 2026-10-31.
FACT: a git child blocks on a FIFO at `objects/info/alternates` (`timeout 3 git rev-list --objects --all` exited 124). That stall sits in a child that `exec.CommandContext` kills, so it ends with the gateway's checkpoint deadline, as a slow co-tenant upload does; the in-process open has no such bound. This design closes the unbounded stall only — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:645-650; pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:87.
OPEN: a FIFO under `objects/` of a repository that carries `lenny.gitStoreUrl` stalls the op lock for up to the checkpoint deadline per attempt. Not filed by G1; a later round decides whether a bounded stall is acceptable.
WATCHOUT: the new race rows must drive the helper rather than the whole capture, because `ArchiveTree` has its own open race: `archiveRoot` calls `os.Open` after `filepath.Walk`'s lstat, and `readChunk(pr, ...)` takes no context, so a file swapped for a FIFO in that window blocks the handler under `s.ops` — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go:97,131; pkg/adapter/workspace/archive.go:68; pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:289,304.
WATCHOUT: the `git config --file` call must not run with its cwd inside a slot. Without `GIT_DIR`, setup discovery runs from the cwd even under `--file`. The adapter's process cwd is outside the slot, so CODE-6 states nothing; do not set `cmd.Dir` to the repository — EVIDENCE: UNVERIFIED beyond the reading of git's RUN_SETUP_GENTLY, no prototype run.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1 WATCHOUT review-log:1761 "self-harm on the agent's own capture; not filed"]: a blocking in-process open happens under `s.ops` and blocks co-tenants, so the effect is not self-harm. The target-parses-as-config argument does not apply to a FIFO.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1 OPEN on bullet (3) `config`]: named this gap first and fixed where to look.

CODE-6 step 2.4 reads "Before indexing each pack" and "Index as §14 rule 3 and CODE-5 step 6 state". The item 11 first-bind table gains "every pack failing `index-pack`" — BECAUSE rounds kept filing one more item 6 twin missing from item 11, and CODE-5 step 6 does not state the retry, so the old pointer's target did not hold it — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's clause (a second copy of item 6's row, the third twin); restating the retry in CODE-5 step 6 (a third statement of rule 3).

DECISION: applied both designs as one edit to TEST-1 item 8 (the `lenny.baseCommit` clause replaced in place, one fail-branch sentence inserted after the `ExtractTree` sentence), with CODE-6 bullet (3) and SPEC-4 Edit (a) untouched — BECAUSE each stays the one home of its rule and only the test rows were missing — ALTERNATIVES: the design's rejected options, recorded in non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1.
WATCHOUT: item 8's fail-branch row must inject exit 1, never 128: exit 128 is bullet (3)'s skip branch, so a seam row at 128 asserts the wrong outcome — EVIDENCE: `git config --file /nonexistent/x --list` exits 128 on git 2.50.1.

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 item 8 pins bullet (3)'s fail branch with three rows (`git config --list` exits 1, fails to start, and a mode-0000 `config`) in one sentence; bullet (3) is not edited — BECAUSE a git-only row passes an implementation that turns every open error into no keys, and that is the likeliest bug since ENOENT and ELOOP need a skip branch — ALTERNATIVES: a stub `git` on PATH (items 7 and 8 already oblige a seam, and a stub depends on PATH lookup); a cancelled context (a later step can fail the operation anyway, so it does not discriminate at operation level); a filesystem-seam open failure (bullet (1) reuses the scrub resolver, which has no such seam).
DECISION: the `lenny.baseCommit` rows are `--all`, a resolving `HEAD`, and absent, all on a repository carrying `lenny.gitStoreUrl`, and CODE-6 bullet (3) stays as written — BECAUSE the CODE-6 lead sentence already sends the implementor to the §13.4 exception, which owns the 40-hex check — ALTERNATIVES: restating the regex in bullet (3), rejected as a second home; deleting `--end-of-options`, rejected as cutting a security defence no finding asks to remove.
FACT: on git 2.50.1, `--all` after `--end-of-options` dies with "option '--all' must come before non-option arguments" (exit 128), and `HEAD HEAD^{tree}` after it exits 0, so only the `HEAD` row discriminates the §13.4 check — EVIDENCE: `git rev-list --objects --not --end-of-options --all` and `git rev-list --count HEAD --not --end-of-options HEAD 'HEAD^{tree}'` run read-only on this worktree, 2026-10-31.
FACT: `git config --file <path> --list` exits 128 for every read failure as well as for a malformed file: a missing path, a closed `/proc/self/fd/N`, and a directory all gave 128, so a non-128 exit never comes from a real git read and needs the seam — EVIDENCE: read-only runs on git 2.50.1, 2026-10-31.
FACT: a mode-0000 `config` survives restore, because `extractRegular` opens the destination with the tar header's permission bits and writes through that descriptor, so this row needs no on-disk tree exception — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/extract.go:87, pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go:195.
FACT: `pkg/adapter` starts no git process today; the "git-runner seam" exists only as items 7 and 8 name it, in new `gitstore.go`, and no CODE deliverable names it — EVIDENCE: lookup for `exec.CommandContext` under pkg/adapter finds only workspace/setup.go; non-spec-changes.md:311,312.
WATCHOUT: a mode-0000 row needs a skip under root, and a skip reason must open with a `cmd/lenny-test/skipreason` category or tier 0 rejects it; `skipAsRoot` is the precedent, in the very package bullet (1) reuses — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/scrub/resolve_linux_test.go:16-21; cmd/lenny-test/skipreason/skipreason.go:28-30.
OPEN: bullet (3)'s "exit 128 carries no keys" also covers a real read failure inside git, because git exits 128 for EIO and for a wrongly inherited descriptor, so that case skips a store-backed repository silently and the new rows cannot see it. Item 10's positive rows catch a wrong descriptor; a transient EIO stays uncovered. `git config --file - --list` with the helper reading the descriptor itself would leave exit 128 meaning malformed only. Not staged: no finding raised it, and it rewrites a rule for a one-off error on an emptyDir.

### [non-spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the warm fill is `Server.FillGitStores(ctx)`, a `PreReadyConfig.FillGitStores` gate in `preReady.run` after `AwaitSupervisor` and ahead of the `p.owned` recheck; the embedded runtimes call it directly after `ConnectGateway` — BECAUSE the gate position takes the fill out of the supervisor's dial window and puts it on the context the signal and not-live paths already cancel, with no new duration or signal code — ALTERNATIVES: rendering `--dial-timeout` (a second derived duration and a millisecond renderer, and SIGTERM stays open); a `ctx` on `ConnectGateway` with a signal goroutine (second signal path, window stays open); `ConnectGateway` inside a gate (reorders forwarder wiring).
WATCHOUT: `FillGitStores` must not log. `preReady.fail` logs only for the exit's owner, so a log inside the fill reports a failure on a clean pod deletion — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/preready.go `fail`.
WATCHOUT: `preReadyConfig` sets the gate in its opening literal, because the function returns at `rt == nil` and an unsupervised sidecar would otherwise report ready with no store — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go `preReadyConfig`.
WATCHOUT: the `gatewayAddr` check (CODE-4 step 1) and the SA token option (step 5) stay in `ConnectGateway`; `FillGitStores` has no `gatewayAddr`.
WATCHOUT: rule M6, INFRA-1 step 3, DOCS-1, item 18, SPEC-2, and the "only bound on a stalled fill" sentence need no edit for the supervisor window. `AwaitSupervisor`'s timeout is spent before the gate, and `boundedWarmUp` wraps only `WarmUp`.
WATCHOUT: embedded runtimes need no signal wiring for the fill: `signal.Notify` runs later, so Go's default SIGTERM action ends them with no SDK up — EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go and cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go, the `signal.Notify` goroutine after `net.Listen`.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]: the Settled line **Fill location** is false. The fill no longer runs inside `ConnectGateway`; it is the `FillGitStores` gate above, and the embedded runtimes call it directly.
FACT: `acceptOneSupervisor` holds the only `net.Listen` on the supervise socket, so the supervisor dial window covers everything before `AwaitSupervisor` — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/supervise.go, supervisor/supervisor.h `SV_DIAL_TIMEOUT_DEFAULT_MS`.

### [non-spec.5.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: the AWS overlay sets `gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes: 2Gi`, the same value as dev and Kind, and DOCS-1's `maxBytes` row states the value is requested on every install — BECAUSE render-values.sh is the one path through both AWS callers (install-lenny.sh, run-e2e.sh) and it also covers `create_cluster=false`, where the node group does not exist — ALTERNATIVES: `disk_size` on `aws_eks_node_group.default` (no effect under `create_cluster=false`, replaces the node group on an existing cluster), a lower chart default (tunes a production default to one provider's root disk), a request conditioned on stores (the chart cannot see stores; a separate request value drifts from the budget Decision 5 ties it to), the GCP and Azure overlays (their node disks fit 20Gi).
WATCHOUT: `gitMirror:` in the AWS overlay goes above `${GATEWAY_SA_BLOCK}`, with `image:`. That line sits at column 0 of the heredoc and expands to an empty string when no IAM role is set, so nothing may depend on following it — EVIDENCE: scripts/cloud/aws/render-values.sh:58-60, :113
FACT: no test reads render-values.sh output; TEST-1 item 22 runs helm template and never sees the overlay. The discriminating check for a Pending gateway on AWS is `helm --wait` and `rollout status` in install-lenny.sh — EVIDENCE: scripts/cloud/aws/install-lenny.sh:49, scripts/cloud/aws/run-e2e.sh:286
UNVERIFIED: the 20 GiB default for an EKS managed node group with no `disk_size` is provider behaviour recalled from memory, and the tree does not state it. deploy/terraform/cloud/aws/cluster.tf:207-223 confirms only that neither `disk_size` nor a launch template is set. A tier 6 AWS run would confirm it.

### [non-spec.5.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: a seed `ownerTenantId` that differs from the stored owner is a §17.6 security-critical field reported by `poolConflicts`, and `upsertPools` applies rule 2's pool clauses on the create branch and on `doUpdate`, each ahead of its `dryRun` guard — BECAUSE the §17.6 row blocks such a field regardless of `--force-update` with exit 1, its "e.g." list is open, and pool `isolationProfile` already sits under it — ALTERNATIVES: rows alone (a differing owner gives `poolConflicts` no conflict, so nothing rejects it); an ordinary conflict rejected in `doUpdate` (a no-force seed would only skip with a WARN); a §17.6 spec edit (a second statement of rule 2).
WATCHOUT: do not add `stored.OwnerTenantID = desired.OwnerTenantID` to `applyPoolSeed` to honour its full-replace comment. An omitted seed owner would clear the owner, and rule 3 would then resolve the pool for every tenant. Item 31's omitted-owner row discriminates this — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap_resources.go `applyPoolSeed`
WATCHOUT: do not run rule 2 beside the seed-only checks above the `Get` in `upsertPools`, and do not put it inside `doUpdate && !opts.dryRun`. The first fails a seed that omits an owned pool's owner and rejects a skipped seed that writes nothing, and the second makes a dry run report an update a real run rejects — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap_resources.go `upsertPools`
CORRECTS [non-spec.2.review-client-surface.1]: the reading of the tree stands until CODE-8 lands, but its "not filed" disposition is superseded. CODE-8 step 2 now has `poolConflicts` report the owner difference.
FACT: `SEED_SECURITY_CRITICAL_FIELD` appears only under pkg/: spec/ and docs/ have no occurrence, so it joined the summary defects list and REC-1 files it — EVIDENCE: lookup exact over spec, docs, and pkg

### [non-spec.5.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: rule M10's `ResolveNetworkError` sentence stays unscoped and item 20 (M10) asserts that error from `Ensure` of a commit — BECAUSE the table names both rows as probes and the typed result separates a probe miss (`UNAVAILABLE`) from a caller's context ending (`DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`) — ALTERNATIVES: scoping the sentence to ref probes needs a second stated error for the local probe, and a binder local-probe-miss row repeats item 16's deleted-commit row.
DECISION: the reflog row's fixture says `git reset --hard` where the design said "reset away" — BECAUSE a `--soft` reset leaves the blob in the index, where `--indexed-objects` still reaches it, so that fixture passes with `--reflog` dropped — ALTERNATIVES: "reset away" as the design wrote it admits the `--soft` fixture.
FACT: the item 10 fixtures hold on git 2.50.1. In a slot with an alternates store, `git checkout <old> -- f` wrote no object, and a commit of it wrote only the commit object. After `reset --hard`, `rev-list --objects --all --reflog --indexed-objects --not <base> <base>^{tree}` lists the blob and the same command without `--reflog` does not. For the staged blob, `--indexed-objects` lists it and the same command without that flag does not. This closes the design's UNVERIFIED on the fixture wording — EVIDENCE: local experiment in a temp directory, this round.
WATCHOUT: `git add` of matching content wrote no local object against a WRITABLE alternates store in the same experiment, and the read-only case the design feared was not run. Keep the `git checkout <commit> -- <path>` fixture and the blob-absent precondition rather than switching to `git add` — EVIDENCE: same experiment; non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 10.
FACT: `upload.ValidationError.Error()` always opens with `upload: ` and includes the reason, so a message built from it fails item 4 whatever literal prefix precedes it; `Detail` carries no such prefix — EVIDENCE: pkg/upload/upload.go `func (e *ValidationError) Error`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/podclaimerror.go `writeUploadArchiveLimitExceeded`.

### [non-spec.5.fix-G5.1]
FACT: the admin and bootstrap `gitRepository` path cannot see an entry-level `mode`: `runtimestore.SharedAsset` carries Type, Ref, Path, Content, and DestPath, and the admin handlers decode with a non-strict `json.NewDecoder`, so the key is dropped at decode. Only the CRD `SharedAsset` has `Mode`, so the refusal lives in the CODE-2 step 4 CEL rule alone. `auth.mode` on `SharedAssetAuth` is a different field.
