EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/runtimes.go `json.NewDecoder(req.Body).Decode(&body)`.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: split the warm fill out of `ConnectGateway` into exported `Server.FillGitStores(ctx)`, run in `cmd/lenny-adapter` as a `PreReadyConfig.FillGitStores` gate between the `AwaitSupervisor` block and the `p.owned` recheck, and called directly with `context.Background()` by both embedded runtimes after `ConnectGateway` — BECAUSE one move closes both findings: the supervisor's dial window ends at `AwaitSupervisor`, and the gate context is the one the signal and not-live paths already cancel — ALTERNATIVES: rendering `--dial-timeout` (a second derived duration, closes only the first finding, and SIGTERM then needs its own context plumbing); a ctx parameter on `ConnectGateway` with an earlier signal goroutine (duplicates `handleSignals`, leaves the window defect); moving `ConnectGateway` into a gate (reorders forwarder wiring for no gain).
DECISION: CODE-4 step 1's empty-`gatewayAddr` check stays in `ConnectGateway`, verbatim — BECAUSE `gatewayAddr` is that method's parameter, `Server.SharedAssets` is set before it runs (cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:390 then :413), and it fails before any gate — ALTERNATIVES: moving it to `FillGitStores` needs a nil-client test for the same fact.
FACT: after the fix "the only bound on a stalled fill" is true of a non-preConnect pod, supervised or not: `AwaitSupervisor`'s timeout applies inside its own ctx (pkg/adapter/supervise.go:96), `boundedWarmUp` only wraps WarmUp, `RunPing` runs on `pingCtx`, and the supervisor has no post-`ready` timer — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:560-570, supervisor/supervisor.c:408.
FACT: SPEC-2 "On a `preConnect` pool the fill runs in `sdk_connecting`" stays true after the move, because the controller projects `sdk_connecting` from a Running pod and the readiness gate holds it there, whatever the adapter's internal order — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:266-272.
FACT: a deletion during a fill never lands on SPEC-2's `failed` sentence: the planner holds a terminating `warming` pod and takes `sdk_connecting → terminated` for a terminating preConnect pod — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/lifecycle/lifecycle.go:170-183 and :274-285; spec §4.7.10 **None**.
WATCHOUT: `preReadyConfig` returns early at `rt == nil`, so a gate assigned after that line never runs on an unsupervised sidecar pod. Set it in the opening literal — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go, `preReadyConfig`.
WATCHOUT: the `p.owned` recheck after the `AwaitSupervisor` block is what stops a warm-up launch once `ExitOnSignal` has decided what to end. The fill gate goes ahead of that recheck, or it needs its own copy — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/preready.go `run`.
WATCHOUT: `cfg.Exit(0)` does not end the process, so `RunPreReady` returns only when `run` returns: a fill that ignores `ctx` holds a clean signal exit until SIGKILL — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/preready.go:84-85, cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go `Exit` closure.
WATCHOUT: both embedded runtimes call `signal.Notify` only after `net.Listen`, so SIGTERM during their fill ends the process by Go's default action (status 2, also as PID 1), which meets §6.1 with no SDK up. Do not move `signal.Notify` earlier for this — EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/preconnect-echo/main.go:134 and :154, cmd/runtimes/echo-embedded/main.go:158 and :178.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]: the Standing context line "**Fill location:** the warm fill runs inside `Server.ConnectGateway`" is retired. The fill is `Server.FillGitStores`, a `RunPreReady` gate on the sidecar and a direct call on the embedded runtimes. Only the GatewayControl dial and CODE-4 step 1's check stay in `ConnectGateway`.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: the AWS overlay sets `gateway.gitMirror.maxBytes: 2Gi` in the `gateway:` block of scripts/cloud/aws/render-values.sh, and DOCS-1 tells operators the value is requested on every install — BECAUSE the overlay is the one path through both AWS callers (install-lenny.sh and run-e2e.sh), and it also covers `create_cluster=false`, where `aws_eks_node_group.default` has count 0 and a terraform disk_size does nothing — ALTERNATIVES: `disk_size` on the node group (forces node-group replacement, adds cost, and misses a BYO cluster); a lower chart default (the 2Gi or 10Gi number only suits one provider's root disk and starves production mirrors); a conditional request (the chart cannot see stores, because Runtimes are registered at runtime); a separate request value (a second number that can drift from the budget Decision 5 ties it to).
DECISION: no new test pins the overlay — BECAUSE install-lenny.sh runs `helm --wait --timeout 10m` and a rollout status on the gateway, so a Pending gateway already fails the AWS tier 6 path, and no test or render harness reads render-values.sh output. TEST-1 item 22 keeps its dev-preset assertion.
FACT: the GCP and Azure render-values.sh overlays have the same `gateway:` block, and their terraform sets no node disk size. They stay out of this fix because the provider defaults (about 100 GB on GKE and 128 GB on AKS) fit 20Gi. EVIDENCE: scripts/cloud/gcp/render-values.sh, scripts/cloud/azure/render-values.sh, and a grep of deploy/terraform/cloud for disk_size.
FACT: the embedded manifests render under presets/dev.yaml, so the `lenny up` path already gets the 2Gi request and needs no overlay. EVIDENCE: Makefile EMBEDDED_DEV_PROFILE and the `helm template ... -f $(EMBEDDED_DEV_PROFILE)` recipe.
UNVERIFIED: the EKS 20 GiB default for Linux node groups and the GKE and AKS default disk sizes are provider behaviour read from memory. The tree holds no copy of them. A tier 6 run on each provider is the check.
WATCHOUT: in render-values.sh the `${GATEWAY_SA_BLOCK}` line sits at column 0 and expands to a two-space block, and may expand to nothing. Put `gitMirror:` above that line, with the `image:` map, so the YAML stays valid either way. EVIDENCE: scripts/cloud/aws/render-values.sh gateway block.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: a seed naming a different `ownerTenantId` on a registered pool is a §17.6 security-critical field: `poolConflicts` appends it to both `conflicts` and `securityCritical` — BECAUSE rule 2 refuses every owner change, the §17.6 row's "e.g." list is open (pool `isolationProfile` already sits under it), and the existing surface gives error, stored row unchanged, and exit 1 with two lines of code — ALTERNATIVES: an ordinary conflict (a no-force seed skips with WARN, so the owner mismatch stays silent and force needs a second rejection path); a staged §17.6 edit naming `ownerTenantId` (unneeded, and this is the non-spec loop).
DECISION: `applyPoolSeed` does not copy the owner, and a force-update that omits the owner keeps it, under CODE-8 step 1's PUT sentence — BECAUSE §17.6 makes the update a PUT, so PUT's omitted-keeps rule governs it. Copying it would clear the owner on an omitted seed, and rule 3 would then serve the pool to every tenant.
DECISION: for an existing pool, rule 2 runs when `resolveExisting` returns `doUpdate`, ahead of the `dryRun` guard, against the merged pool. The checks at the top of `upsertPools` read the seed alone, so a rule 2 check placed there rejects a legitimate owner-omitting seed. A skip writes nothing and takes no check.
FACT: the §17.6 Update cell and its docs mirrors (lenny-ctl.md, installation.md) stay true for a rule 2 rejection, because seed validation already errors per entry ahead of that cell, and their "Security-critical field change" row covers the owner. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap_resources.go:26-100; docs/operator-guide/lenny-ctl.md:111
CORRECTS [ledger line 1026, `poolConflicts` WATCHOUT]: the tree fact holds until CODE-8 lands, but "drift, not filed" no longer stands: CODE-8 step 2 now makes that seed an error.
USEFUL [ledger line 1065, `applyPoolSeed` copies named fields only]: it confirmed the owner stays out of `applyPoolSeed` with no code change there.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G4.1]

DECISION: rule M10's sentence "A probe waiting on its limiter that misses its end returns a `workspaceplan.ResolveError` with `ResolveNetworkError`" stays unscoped, and the new item 20 row asserts that error from `Ensure` — BECAUSE the handler needs a typed result to tell a probe miss (UNAVAILABLE) from a caller's context ending (DEADLINE_EXCEEDED), the probe's `DefaultTimeout` context would otherwise surface as a bare context error, and a binder `Ensure` failure cannot leak `GIT_CLONE_REF_RESOLVE_TRANSIENT` because `writeRefResolveError` is reached only from the plan pin at pkg/gateway/sessionserver/workspace_plan.go:79 — ALTERNATIVES: scoping the sentence to ref probes, rejected because the local probe would then need a second stated error type.
DECISION: CODE-5 step 3 names `ve.Detail` in place of `ve.Error()` — BECAUSE `ValidationError.Error()` always opens with `upload: ` (pkg/upload/upload.go), so rewording only the literal prefix fails item 4's new assertion — ALTERNATIVES: a separate error type for the size refusal, rejected because it would add a type and split the single `*upload.ValidationError` mapping.
WATCHOUT: a capture-row fixture built with `git add` may not produce a store-only blob. From memory of git's `freshen_file`, `git add` of content that exists only in a read-only alternate writes a local loose object when `utime` fails, so the row would pass without testing the listing. Staging with `git checkout <pre-baseCommit commit> -- <path>` writes no object. Item 10's new rows make each fixture assert the blob's absence from the slot's own object directory first. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 10 and the store modes in item 7 (0755 and 0444 after fill).
UNVERIFIED: the `freshen_file` behaviour above and `--indexed-objects` against the scratch git directory with no `config` were reasoned, not run. This task may create no file except this shard, so no git repository was built. The item 10 rows prove both when implemented.
CORRECTS [round 5 finding "Capture-copy rows never exercise the reflog and index halves"]: its claim that item 11's reflog row "also passes once the shared listing loses the flag" is wrong for a shared listing. That row expects `FailedPrecondition`, and dropping `--reflog` makes the resume succeed, so the row fails. The finding stands for `--indexed-objects` and for a capture listing kept separate from the connectivity listing.
OPEN: CODE-6 helper bullet (4) writes `objects/info/alternates` with `openat`, and it does not say the write refuses a restored symlink at that name. A write through an in-tree symlink would land in the slot and leave the link in place. Not in this group; the security lens should decide whether the overwrite is an unlink plus `O_CREAT|O_EXCL`.

### [non-spec.5.review-performance.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: the unconditional 20Gi gateway ephemeral-storage request exceeds the node disk of this repo's AWS node group, and the AWS overlay is not staged — BECAUSE it leaves the gateway Pending on a cloud install with or without repository stores, where the shipped chart schedules — ALTERNATIVES: did not re-file cold clones behind fetches (open decision 1), Postgres-failover fills, sustained warm rate, pack count, or per-call apiserver reads, all already refuted or accepted.
FACT: `aws_eks_node_group.default` sets no `disk_size` and no launch template, so it takes the EKS default of 20 GiB for Linux nodes (the default is provider and API behaviour, read from memory and not checked in this tree). `render-values.sh` sets no gateway resources or gitMirror values. EVIDENCE: deploy/terraform/cloud/aws/cluster.tf:207-223, scripts/cloud/aws/render-values.sh:108-113
FACT: the gateway's cluster client takes --cluster-qps/--cluster-burst (100/200), so rule P1's GETs do not meet client-go's 5 QPS default. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:158-173
FACT: `tenantRegistered` returns read errors rather than false, so a Postgres failover cannot make rule M8 purge mirrors. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/auth_seams.go:72-81
FACT: Resume and FinalizeWorkspace take no `s.ops`; only checkpoint and interrupt do, so a restore-completion FetchGitPack does not stall co-tenants on that lock. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:154, pkg/adapter/lifecycle.go:60
UNVERIFIED: the pod-side `index-pack --strict` of a full-history warm pack runs under the pool's resource class, because applyResources stamps every container alike, and neither CODE-4 nor INFRA-1 phase (g) measures it. Whoever runs the spike should record the pod-side peak for the smallest class that hosts a store. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/resources.go:15-21
USEFUL [Settled "Tier 3 sizing"]: gave the replica and claim rates without re-deriving them.

### [non-spec.5.review-plan-conformance.1]
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-plan-conformance.2]: the only claimed plan item is still "Proposal 0077, re-derived" (gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:3465-3483), and the deliverable map holds after the rounds 3 to 4 edits, which touched only TEST-1 item 8 of the non-spec text. No plan-conformance finding in round 5.
FACT: the plan-wide rules (INV-1 to INV-7, S-1 to S-7) assign 0111 no deliverable: S-2 does name schemas/lenny-adapter.proto, the file CODE-3 edits, but it is a sequencing rule rather than a deliverable, and the §10.2 "next proposal that edits X" riders stay outside the claimed step and are summary open decision 3. EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:489-557, :2078-2105

### [non-spec.5.review-reliability.1]
CORRECTS [review-log.md:1431 FACT]: "a long fill does not trip the warm-up or supervisor wait bounds" holds only for the adapter's own waits. The supervisor's dial window does trip: supervisor/supervisor.h:43 defaults `--dial-timeout` to 10 minutes, no renderer passes the flag (podspec/supervisor.go:222), and the adapter binds CH-SUPERVISE only inside AwaitSupervisor (pkg/adapter/supervise.go:121), after ConnectGateway returns.
FACT: SIGTERM during a fill sits unread in the `signals` channel, because RunPreReady starts the handler after ConnectGateway; prestop then waits out its timeout. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:150-156, :413, :516; pkg/adapter/prestop/prestop.go:97-107
FACT: a Checkpoint attempt is bounded by the gateway's attemptContext (checkpoint.CheckpointTimeout at the latest), so a git child under stream.Context is killed at that bound. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:643-651
WATCHOUT: git blocks on a FIFO at `objects/info/alternates` or `objects/pack/*.idx` (prototyped; rev-list hit a 3 s timeout on both). Bullets (1) and (2) do not cover those entries. The capture attempt fails at the bound above; the stall is bounded and agent-caused, so it was not filed. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:191-192
UNVERIFIED: CODE-5 step 5 gives fetcher calls the RPC context, but nothing names the context of the adapter's local `git checkout`, while §7.1 **Bind bounds.** covers the adapter's work for the RPC. Not filed: the checkout is local and finite. The implementor should pass it.
USEFUL [review-log.md:1430 FACT]: replacement churn on failing fills is bounded by the controller's create limiter, which closed the stampede lead.

### [non-spec.5.review-security.1]
FACT: the tenant pin survives every recycle (spec/05 "The pin persists across the recycle-to-idle edge", and "A pod on a pool that sets the field is never reused across tenants"), so a lineage grant that outlives its session on a pod cannot reach another tenant. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:459,469.
FACT: `poolPolicyMirror.PoolPolicy` is a direct `poolstore.Get` with no cache, so CODE-8 step 3's "no mirror row means no owner" has no lag window for an owned pool. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:54-61.
FACT: a ref `want` never reaches git argv: `Resolve` runs `ls-remote -- <url>` and matches the ref locally through `pickSHA`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/provisioning/gitref/gitref.go:77-87.
UNVERIFIED: rule I1 binds the pod name only, and `TokenReviewVerifier.VerifyUser` already returns the namespace-bearing username. A same-named pod in another namespace would pass I1, but it needs a verified client certificate, the audience, and a name an agent cannot choose, so this lens judged it hypothetical hardening and did not file it. A later R14 or GatewayControl identity change should bind namespace as well. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/satoken_verify.go:70-98.
UNVERIFIED: §12.9 maps workspace files to T3 with encryption at rest, and the private mirror sits on a gateway `emptyDir`. Agent-pod workspaces share that posture, and a meaning search over §4, §12, and §13 found no rule for node-local volumes, so it was not filed. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md section 12.9 controls table.

### [non-spec.5.review-single-source.1]
DECISION: no (g) finding this round — BECAUSE the only change since the r3 prefix is TEST-1 item 8's `lenny.baseCommit` row, which names cases and cites the §13.4 40-hex check, and a cross-file walk of the rule inventory and of summary reasons against Non-goals and deliverables found one stating site per rule, plus one-clause citers — ALTERNATIVES: filing Decision 5 and the stateless-relay non-goal, and the CPU-quota reason at CODE-3 step 9 and rule M10, rejected because the second site of each is one clause.
FACT: the staged 10 seconds in §7.1 **Bind bounds.** rule 1 equals `2*watchdog.DefaultTickInterval` in CODE-9 step 1 (the interval is 5s), and summary open decision 2's "about 20 GiB plus 18 x S" equals rule M7's sum at D = 1 GiB, so neither pair disagrees. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:181; non-spec-changes.md:122, :132-139; summary.md:131
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-single-source.1]: its "do not re-file" reasons for the clause pairs held and saved two false findings.

### [non-spec.5.review-test-coverage.1]
FACT: the gateway ClusterRole already grants get on sandboxwarmpools, sandboxtemplates, sandboxes, and create on tokenreviews, so rule P1's injected cluster reader hides no RBAC gap. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:188,237
FACT: the create-and-start path has no pre-attached retry loop today, because a slot reserved at create takes BindReservedSlot with no retry; §7.1 Bind bounds rule 2's "each attempt" has nothing to pin yet, so no item 40 row was filed for it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:43-48; create_and_start.go:447-474
FACT: `not-yet-applicable:` and `blocked:` are both admitted skip categories, and skipAsRoot exists as item 8 says. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-test/skipreason/skipreason.go:28,30; pkg/adapter/scrub/resolve_linux_test.go:17
FACT: writePodClaimError's archiveLimit case sits ahead of SlotFailedError handling, so a ValidateTree refusal on a concurrent-workspace bind keeps UPLOAD_ARCHIVE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/podclaimerror.go:182-197
WATCHOUT: applyPoolSeed and poolConflicts carry no owner today, so a force-update with a different ownerTenantId is silently ignored unless CODE-8 adds it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap_resources.go:143-165,174

### [f2.other-proposals.0112-reader]
DECISION: the 0112 row of summary.md "## Impacts on other proposals" stays and gains two facts: `vcsTokenFromVerdict`'s not-found text also uses the `vcsTokenKey` constant, and the direct two-argument `Resolve` calls in `cmd/lenny-gateway/secret_probe_test.go` change with the reworked subjects. The status, the sequencing question, and "Nothing is required of 0112's implementor" stand as written. EVIDENCE: 0111 non-spec-changes.md:215; impl/0112-credential-namespace:cmd/lenny-gateway/secret_probe.go:66,97,121; impl/0112-credential-namespace:cmd/lenny-gateway/secret_probe_test.go:240.
OPEN: the key parameter also breaks the `SecretReader` fakes `failingSecrets` in tests/tier3_contract/vcs_credential_read/vcs_credential_read_test.go and `flakySecrets` in tests/tier9_security/vcs_credential_read_failclosed_test.go on impl/0112-credential-namespace. Neither CODE-7 nor the row names them, the failure is a compile error for 0111's implementor rather than a 0112 obligation, and this firing's gate left adding them to CODE-7's test edits to a later pass.
